Cohomological Milnor formula and Saito’s conjecture on characteristic classes Thanks: † Enlin Yang is the corresponding author. Thanks: † yangenlin@math.pku.edu.cn Thanks: † School of Mathematical Sciences, Peking University, No.5 Yiheyuan Road Haidian District., Beijing, 100871, P.R. China. Thanks: ‡ zhaoyigeng@westlake.edu.cn Thanks: ‡ Institute for Theoretical Sciences, Westlake University, No.600 Dunyu Road, Xihu District, Hangzhou, Zhejiang, 310040, P.R. China.
Enlin Yang †, Yigeng Zhao ‡

Abstract
. We confirm the quasi-projective case of Saito’s conjecture [Invent.Math.207,597–695 (2017)], namely that the cohomological characteristic classes defined by Abbes and Saito can be computed in terms of the characteristic cycles. We construct a cohomological characteristic class supported on the non-acyclicity locus of a separated morphism relatively to a constructible sheaf. As applications of the functorial properties of this class, we prove cohomological analogs of the Milnor formula and the conductor formula for constructible sheaves on (not necessarily smooth) varieties.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 14F20; Secondary 14C17, 11S15.

1.
Introduction

1.1.

Let
h:X\to{\rm Spec}k
be a separated morphism of finite type over a perfect field
k
. Let
\Lambda
be a finite local ring such that the characteristic of the residue field is invertible in
k
.
Let
\mathcal{K}_{X/k}=Rh^{!}\Lambda
.
For a constructible complex
\mathcal{F}
of
\Lambda
-modules of finite tor-dimension on
X
, the cohomological characteristic class
C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})\in H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k})
is introduced by Abbes and Saito in
[
1
]
by using Verdier pairing (cf.
[
11
]
)
1
1

1

See also
[
16
]
for the transcendental setting.
.
Using ramification theory, Abbes and Saito calculate the cohomological characteristic classes for rank 1 sheaves under certain ramification conditions in
[
1
]
. However, the calculation for general constructible étale sheaves remains an outstanding question in ramification theory.
Later, using the singular support defined by Beilinson
[
4
]
,
Saito
[
22
]
constructs the characteristic cycles of constructible étale sheaves and further proposes the following conjecture.

Conjecture 1.2

(Saito,
[
22
, Conjecture 6.8.1]
)
.

Let
X
be a closed sub-scheme of a smooth scheme over a perfect field
k
.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be a constructible complex of
\Lambda
-modules of finite tor-dimension on
X
.
Let
cc_{X}(\mathcal{F}):=cc_{X,0}(\mathcal{F})\in{\rm CH}_{0}(X)
be the characteristic class defined in
[
22
, Definition 6.7.2]
via the characteristic cycle of
\mathcal{F}
.
Then we have

(1.2.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})={\rm cl}(cc_{X}(\mathcal{F}))\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}),

where
{\rm cl}:{\rm CH}_{0}(X)\to H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k})
is the cycle class map.

Saito’s conjecture says that the cohomological characteristic class can be computed in terms of the characteristic cycle.
Note that the two involved ramification invariants in Conjecture
1.2
are defined in quite different ways. The characteristic cycle is characterized by the Milnor formula
[
22
, Theorem 5.9]
, while the cohomological characteristic class in some sense is defined via the categorical trace (cf. Section
3
).
In the characteristic zero case, the equality (
1.2.1
) on a complex manifold is the microlocal index formula proved by Kashiwara and Schapira
[
16
, 9.5.1]
. However, we don’t know such a microlocal description for characteristic cycles in positive characteristic
2
2

2

Abe
[
2
]
obtains certain microlocal descriptions for characteristic cycles using
\infty
-categories.
.
One of our main goals of this paper is to show the quasi-projective case of Saito’s conjecture.

Theorem 1.3

(Theorem
6.13
)
.

Conjecture
1.2
holds for any smooth and quasi-projective scheme
X
over a perfect field
k
of characteristic
p>0
.

When
X
is projective and smooth over a finite field
k
of characteristic
p
, the cohomology group
H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k})
is highly non-trivial. For example, if
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{m}
with
\ell\neq p
, then we have

H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k})\simeq H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{m})^{\vee}\simeq\pi_{1}^{\rm ab}(X)/\ell^{m}
(cf.
[
21
, Theorem 1.12]
).

1.4.

Our approach to Saito’s conjecture is the fibration method, which leans on the construction of the relative version of cohomological characteristic classes over a general base scheme.
To achieve this, it is necessary to impose additional transversality conditions on the structure morphism. Let
S
be a Noetherian scheme and
{\rm Sch}_{S}
the category of separated schemes of finite type over
S
.
Let
\Lambda
be a Noetherian ring such that
m\Lambda=0
for some integer
m
invertible on
S
.
Let
h:X\to S
be a separated morphism of finite type,
\mathcal{K}_{X/S}=Rh^{!}\Lambda
and
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
.
In fact, under certain smooth and transversality conditions on
h
, we introduce the relative (cohomological) characteristic class
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})\in H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})
in
[
30
, Definition 3.6]
.
It is further generalized
to any separated morphism
h:X\to S
which is (universally) locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
by using categorical traces
[
18
, 2.20]
.
We also define relative characteristic classes in a more general case that only assumes local acyclicity away from small closed subsets. Indeed, if
Z\subseteq X
is a closed subscheme
such that
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{S}})=H^{1}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{S}})=0
, and
if
X\setminus Z\to S
is (universally) locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
, then the relative characteristic class
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})\in H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})
remains well-defined (cf. Definition
3.5
).
We prove the following fibration formula for cohomological characteristic classes.

Theorem 1.5

(Theorem
6.5
)
.

Let
Y
be a smooth connected curve over a perfect field
k
of characteristic
p>0
.
Let
\Lambda
be a finite local ring such that the characteristic of the residue field is invertible in
k
.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a separated morphism of finite type and
Z\subseteq|X|
be a finite set of closed points.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that
f|_{X\setminus Z}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
.
Then we have

(1.5.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=c_{1}(f^{\ast}\Omega_{Y/k}^{1,\vee})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})-\sum_{x\in Z}{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}}(\mathcal{F},f)\cdot[x]\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}),

where
R\Phi(\mathcal{F},f)
is the complex of vanishing cycles and
{\rm dimtot}={\rm dim}+{\rm Sw}
is the total dimension.

As a consequence, we have the following cohomological version of Grothendieck-Ogg-Shafarevich formula:

Corollary 1.6

(Corollary
6.6
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth connected curve over a perfect field
k
of characteristic
p>0
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
and
Z\subseteq X
a finite set of closed points such that the cohomology sheaves of
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
are locally constant. Then we have

(1.6.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})={\rm rank}\mathcal{F}\cdot c_{1}(\Omega_{X/k}^{1,\vee})-\sum_{x\in Z}{a}_{x}(\mathcal{F})\cdot[x]\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}),

where
a_{x}(\mathcal{F})={\rm rank}\mathcal{F}|_{\bar{\eta}}-{\rm rank}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{x}}+{\rm Sw}_{x}\mathcal{F}
is the Artin conductor and
\eta
is the generic point of
X
.

Corollary
1.6
confirms Saito’s conjecture
1.2
in the curve case.
The quasi-projective case of Saito’s conjecture
1.2
follows from Theorem
1.5
by using the fibration method.

1.7.

For the proof of Theorem
1.5
, the key observation is that

C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})-c_{1}(f^{\ast}\Omega_{Y/k}^{1,\vee})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})
is zero on
X\setminus Z
(cf. Theorem
3.13
). Hence the difference comes from a cohomology class in
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/k})
. In this paper we construct a canonical lifting of
C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})-c_{1}(f^{\ast}\Omega_{Y/k}^{1,\vee})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})
in
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/k})
, where
Z
contains the non-acyclicity locus of
f:X\to Y
relatively to
\mathcal{F}
.
Later for proving a cohomological version of the Milnor formula
[
22
, Theorem 5.9]
, we need this lifting in a relative version as follows.

1.8.

Consider a commutative diagram in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
:

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.77083pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.77083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 69.84026pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern where
\tau:Z\to X
is a closed immersion and
g
is a smooth morphism of relative dimension
r
.
We define an object
\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}
on
X
sitting in a distinguished triangle (cf. (
4.2.13
))

(1.8.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}\to\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\to\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}\xrightarrow{+1}.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that
X\setminus Z\to Y
is universally locally acyclic relatively
to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
and that
X\to S
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
.
We introduce a new cohomological characteristic class
\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\in H_{Z}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})
supported on
Z
in Definition
4.6
,
which measures the difference

C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})-c_{r}(f^{*}\Omega_{Y/S}^{1,\vee})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})
.
If
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{Y}})=H^{1}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{Y}})=0
, then

H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/S})\simeq H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})
and

\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
defines a class in
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/S})
, which is denoted by
C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
.
We have the following formula:

Theorem 1.9

(Fibration formula, Theorem
5.1
)
.

If
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{Y}})=H^{1}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{Y}})=0
,
then we have

(1.9.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})=c_{r}(f^{*}\Omega_{Y/S}^{1,\vee})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})+C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S}).

We call
C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
(resp.
\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
) the non-acyclicity class of
\mathcal{F}
. We expect that this class on (not necessarily smooth) varieties could play a role as the characteristic cycles on smooth varieties.

If
Z
is empty, we prove (
1.9.1
) in Theorem
3.13
.
In the case where
f={\rm id}
and
S={\rm Spec}k
, the cohomology sheaves of
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
are locally constant on
X\setminus Z
since
{\rm id}:X\setminus Z\to X\setminus Z
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
. Then (
1.9.1
) is Abbes-Saito’s localization formula
[
1
, Proposition 5.2.3]
.

1.10.

Now we summarize the functorial properties for the non-acyclicity classes.
For simplicity, we denote such a diagram (
1.8
) by
\Delta=\Delta^{Z}_{X/Y/S}

and
\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
by
C_{\Delta}(\mathcal{F})
.

Proposition 1.11

(Proposition
4.11
and Proposition
4.14
)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
.
Assume that
Y\to S
is smooth,
X\setminus Z\to Y
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
and that
X\to S
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
.

(1)

Let
b:S^{\prime}\to S
be a morphism of Noetherian schemes. Let
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{Z^{\prime}}
be the base change of
\Delta=\Delta^{Z}_{X/Y/S}
by
b:S^{\prime}\to S
. Let
b_{X}:X^{\prime}=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to X
be the base change of
b
by
X\to S
. Then
we have

(1.11.1)

\displaystyle b_{X}^{\ast}C_{\Delta}(\mathcal{F})=C_{\Delta^{\prime}}(b_{X}^{\ast}\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}),

where
b_{X}^{\ast}:H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})\to H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
is the induced pull-back morphism.

(2)

Consider a diagram
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}
. Let
s:X\to X^{\prime}
be a proper morphism over
Y
such that
Z\subseteq s^{-1}(Z^{\prime})
.
Then
we have

(1.11.2)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}(C_{\Delta}(\mathcal{F}))=C_{\Delta^{\prime}}(Rs_{\ast}\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}),

where
s_{\ast}:H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})\to H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S})
is the induced push-forward morphism.

If
Z
is a finite set of closed points, the non-acyclicity class can be calculated as follows.

Theorem 1.12

(Theorem
6.2
)
.

Let
Y
be a smooth curve over a perfect field
k
of characteristic
p>0
.
Let
\Lambda
be a finite local ring such that the characteristic of the residue field is invertible in
k
. Let
f:X\to Y
be a separated morphism of finite type and
x\in|X|
a closed point. Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that
f|_{X\setminus\{x\}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus\{x\}}
.
Then we have

(1.12.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/Y/k}^{\{x\}}(\mathcal{F})=-{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}}(\mathcal{F},f)\quad{\rm in}\quad\Lambda=H^{0}_{x}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}).

The above theorem gives a cohomological analog of the Milnor formula proved by Saito in
[
22
, Theorem 5.9]
, and does not involve the smoothness assumption on
X
.
Its proof is based on the pull-back property of the non-acyclicity classes (cf. Proposition
1.11
.(1)) together with the method in
[
3
]
.
Theorem
1.5
follows from (
1.9.1
) together with the cohomological Milnor formula (
1.12.1
).
The construction of non-acyclicity classes in this paper can be applied to motivic categories
with six-functor formalism.
If there is a generalization of the argument in
[
3
]
to the
p
-adic cohomology theory (cf.
[
3
, 3.2]
), we could expect that the construction will lead to a solution of Deligne’s conjecture on the Milnor formula in the mixed characteristic case
[
7
, Conjecture 1.9]
.

1.13.

As an application of Proposition
1.11
.(2), we prove a cohomological version of the conductor formula
[
25
, Theorem 2.2.3]
(also of Bloch’s conjecture on the conductor formula for constant sheaves
[
5
]
).

Theorem 1.14

(Theorem
6.7
)
.

Let
Y
be a smooth connected curve over a perfect field
k
of characteristic
p>0
.
Let
\Lambda
be a finite local ring such that the characteristic of the residue field is invertible in
k
.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a proper morphism over
k
and
y\in|Y|
a closed point. Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
. Assume that
f|_{X\setminus f^{-1}(y)}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus f^{-1}(y)}
.
Then we have

(1.14.1)

\displaystyle f_{\ast}\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/k}^{f^{-1}(y)}(\mathcal{F})=-{a}_{y}(Rf_{\ast}\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad\Lambda=H^{0}_{y}(Y,\mathcal{K}_{Y/k}).

In
[
25
, Theorem 2.2.3]
, Saito assumes that
f
is projective since the global index formula for the characteristic cycle is only known for projective schemes
3
3

3

Recently, Abe
[
2
]
proves the index formula for proper schemes by using
\infty
-categories.
. However, using the proper push-forward property for cohomological characteristic classes, we could drop this assumption in Theorem
1.14
. This is one of the advantages by using cohomological arguments.

1.15.

We derive the cohomological Milnor formula from the pull-back property and a deformation property of constructible étale sheaves together with a simple fact that
C_{X/X/k}^{\{x\}}(\mathcal{G}_{x})={\rm rank}\mathcal{G}_{x}
for any smooth scheme
X
of dimension
\geq 1
and any constructible sheaf
\mathcal{G}_{x}
supported on a closed point
x\in|X|
. The cohomological conductor formula follows from the proper push-forward property. It would be interesting to see whether the non-acyclicity classes are characterized by Proposition
1.11
and the above formula for skyscraper constructible sheaves.

1.16.

This article is organized as follows.
In Section
2
, we introduce the definition of transversality conditions, which is crucial for defining the non-acyclicity classes.
In Section
3
, we review the construction of the relative characteristic class and its functorial properties. Then we prove (
1.9.1
) under the condition
Z=\emptyset
in Theorem
3.13
.
In Section
4
, we construct the non-acyclicity classes and
study their functorial properties. Then we prove the fibration formula (
1.9.1
)
in Section
5
.
In Section
6
, we show the cohomological Milnor formula (
1.12.1
) and deduce the cohomological conductor formula (
1.14.1
). As an application of the cohomological Milnor formula, we establish an induction formula for the cohomological characteristic classes and then prove the quasi-projective case of Saito’s conjecture in Theorem
6.13
.

Notation and Conventions

(1)

Let
S
be a Noetherian scheme and
{\rm Sch}_{S}
the category of separated schemes of finite type over
S
.
Let
\Lambda
be a Noetherian ring such that
m\Lambda=0
for some integer
m
invertible on
S
unless otherwise stated explicitly.

(2)

For any scheme
X\in{\rm Sch}_{S}
,
we denote by
D(X,\Lambda)
the derived category of étale sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on
X
and by

D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
the derived category of complexes of étale sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules of finite tor-dimension with constructible cohomology groups on
X
.

(3)

For any morphism
f:X\to Y
in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
, we denote by
D_{\rm ctf}(X/Y,\Lambda)\subseteq D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
the full subcategory consisting of objects
\mathcal{F}
such that
f
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
(also say
\mathcal{F}
is universally locally acyclic over
Y
). We define
K_{0}(X/Y,\Lambda)
to be the Grothendieck group of
D_{\rm ctf}(X/Y,\Lambda)
.

(4)

For any separated morphism
f:X\to Y
in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
, we use the following notation

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}=Rf^{!}\Lambda,\quad D_{X/Y}(-)=R\mathcal{H}om(-,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}).

(5)

For
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm ctf}(Y,\Lambda)
on
S
-schemes
X
and
Y
respectively,
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}\mathcal{G}
denotes
{\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}{\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{G}
on
X\times_{S}Y
.

(6)

To simplify our notation, we omit to write
R
or
L
to denote the derived functors unless otherwise stated explicitly or for
R\mathcal{H}om
.

2.
Transversality condition

2.1.

In this section, we introduce a generalized version of the transversality condition studied
in
[
22
]
and investigate its relation with universal local acyclicity. This generalized version is crucial for defining the non-acyclicity classes.
Consider the following cartesian diagram in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
:

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 10.12234pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 40.1441pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-8.41667pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 15.5993pt\raise-25.90276pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.41667pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.41667pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(Y,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm ctf}(T,\Lambda)
.
Recall that there is a base change morphism

(2.1.5)

p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{}i^{!}f^{\ast}\mathcal{G},

which is adjoint to

i_{!}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\rm b.c}f^{\ast}\delta_{!}\delta^{!}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{{\rm adj}}f^{\ast}\mathcal{G}
.
We define a morphism
c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}

to be the composition

(2.1.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}:i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\mathcal{G}&\xrightarrow{id\otimes\eqref{eq:pullbackforanyGdefTrans}}i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}i^{!}f^{\ast}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{(a)}i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\mathcal{G}),\end{split}

where
(a)
is adjoint to the following composition (cf.
[
22
, (8.8)]
)

(2.1.7)

i_{!}(i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}i^{!}f^{\ast}\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\rm proj.formula}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}i_{!}i^{!}f^{\ast}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\rm adj}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\mathcal{G}.

Note that if
i={\rm id}
and
\delta
is a closed immersion, then
c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
is induced by (cf. definition of (
2.1.5
))

(2.1.8)

\displaystyle\delta^{!}\simeq\delta^{\ast}\delta_{!}\delta^{!}\xrightarrow{\rm adj}\delta^{\ast}.

We also put
c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F}}:=c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F},\Lambda}:i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\to i^{!}\mathcal{F}
.
If
c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F}}
is an isomorphism, then we say the cartesian diagram (
2.1
) is
\mathcal{F}
-transversal
(and also say that the morphism
\delta
is
\mathcal{F}
-transversal
).
For the special case where
f={\rm id}
in (
2.1
), it agrees with Saito’s definition
[
22
, Definition 8.5]
.
The basic properties of
c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
are summarized in the following two lemmas.

Lemma 2.2
.

By
[
26
, Lemma 1.1.2.(2)]
(and its proof), the diagram (1) is commutative.
Applying the functor
c_{s,id,i^{\ast}\mathcal{F},-}
to the base change morphism
p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\mathcal{G}\to i^{!}f^{\ast}\mathcal{G}
, we get the commutativity of the diagram (2).
The diagram (3) is commutative by the functoriality of the base change morphism (
2.1.5
).
∎

where the upper diagram is commutative by applying (
(2)
) to
\mathcal{H}=g_{\ast}\mathcal{F}
, the lower diagram is commutative by applying
c_{\delta,fg,-,\mathcal{G}}
to

g^{\ast}g_{\ast}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
.

∎

The following proposition generalizes
[
22
, Lemma 8.6.1 and Corollary 8.10]
.

Proposition 2.4
.

Consider the cartesian diagram (
2.1
). Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(Y,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm ctf}(T,\Lambda)
.

(1)

Assume that
\delta
is a smooth morphism of relative dimension
d
.
Consider the following canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle t_{i,\mathcal{F}}:i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\Lambda(d)[2d]\to i^{!}\mathcal{F}

defined by the Poincaré duality
[

, Théorème 3.2.5]

(

[

, (8.3)]
)
. Then we have a commutative diagram

-transversal.

is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
. Then

is an isomorphism. In particular,
\delta
is
\mathcal{F}
-transversal.

where
(a)
is the base change for the trace map,

),

.(3) and the fact that

,

is commutative by

, Lemma 1.1.2.(1)]
.

,
we may assume that

is a closed immersion. We put

. Consider the following cartesian diagram

Since
f
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
,
f
is also strongly locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
.
By
[
22
, Proposition 8.9]
, we obtain an isomorphism
\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}j_{\ast}j^{\ast}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\simeq}j^{\prime}_{\ast}j^{\prime\ast}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\mathcal{G})
.
Note that
i_{\ast}(i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\mathcal{G})\simeq\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}i_{\ast}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\mathcal{G}\simeq\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\delta_{\ast}\delta^{!}\mathcal{G}
.
Now we consider the following diagram between distinguished triangles:

The commutativity of the above diagram can be verified by using the proof of
[
26
, Lemma 1.1.3]
.
Thus
i_{\ast}(c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}})
is an isomorphism.
Since
i
is a closed immersion, the morphism
c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
is also an isomorphism.

∎

The following proposition gives a converse of Proposition
2.4
,
which will not be used in this paper.

Proposition 2.5

(Saito,
[
22
, Proposition 8.11]
)
.

Let
f:Y\to T
be a morphism of schemes of finite type over a field
k

of characteristic
p
and let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(Y,\Lambda)
. The morphism
f
is locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
if the following condition is satisfied:

Let
T^{\prime}
and
W
be smooth schemes over a finite extension of
k
, and

be a cartesian diagram of schemes where

is proper and generically finite and

is

-transversal.

There is a canonical morphism

(2.6.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\otimes^{L}\delta^{!}\Lambda\to\Lambda

defined by the following composition:

(2.6.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\otimes^{L}\delta^{!}\Lambda=\delta^{\ast}{\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}g^{!}\Lambda\otimes\delta^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{c_{\delta,{\rm id},{\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}g^{!}\Lambda}}\delta^{!}({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}g^{!}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\delta^{!}(\eqref{eq:pullbackforanyGdefTrans})}\delta^{!}({\rm pr}_{2}^{!}g^{\ast}\Lambda)=\Lambda.

If

is a smooth morphism, then

is an isomorphism by the
{\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}
-transversality of
\delta
(cf.
[
22
, Lemma 8.6.2]
).

45.01697pt\raise-62.55554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.01697pt\raise-62.55554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern where
\delta_{0}
and
\delta_{1}
are the diagonal morphisms.
Assume that

\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}=g^{!}\Lambda
is locally constant and that
\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\otimes^{L}\delta^{!}\Lambda\simeq\Lambda

(
cf. (
2.6.5
)
)
. Then we have

(1)

The morphism
f
is
\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}
-transversal, i.e.,
\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}=f^{!}\Lambda\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f^{!}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}=\mathcal{K}_{X/S}
is an isomorphism.

(2)

The composition

(2.7.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda=f^{!}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow[\simeq]{c_{f,{\rm id},\delta^{!}\Lambda,\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}}}f^{!}(\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\otimes^{L}\delta^{!}\Lambda)\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{eq:KYSLambda}}f^{!}\Lambda=\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}

is an isomorphism.

(3)

If
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/Y,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/Y,\Lambda)
, then
\delta
is

\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
-transversal, i.e.,

(2.7.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{Y}^{L}D_{X/Y}(\mathcal{G})\overset{(a)}{\simeq}i^{\ast}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{G}))\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\to i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{G}))

is an isomorphism, where
(a)
is induced by the following composition:

(2.7.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}D_{X/Y}(\mathcal{G})=R\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}_{X/Y})&\xleftarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{eq:KXYSfdelta}}R\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\otimes f^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda)\\
&\simeq R\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{K}_{X/S})\otimes f^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda=D_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda.\end{split}

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}
is locally constant, the assertion (1) follows from the proof of
[
22
, Lemma 8.6.2]
.
The second claim follows from the fact that
\delta^{!}\Lambda
is locally constant.
Now we prove (3). Since
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/Y,\Lambda)
, we have
D_{X/Y}(\mathcal{G})\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/Y,\Lambda)
by
[
18
, Corollary 2.18]
. Since

D_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})\simeq D_{X/Y}(\mathcal{G})\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}
, the morphism
f:X\to Y
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
D_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
. By
[
15
, Corollary 2.5]
,
the morphism
f\times f:X\times_{S}X\to Y\times_{S}Y
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
. By Proposition
2.4
.(2),
\delta
is
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
-transversal.
∎

2.8.

Now we compare the morphism induced by
c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F}}
on cohomology groups with the Gysin map. Before that, let us
briefly recall the cycle class defined by a regular immersion.
Let
\eta:P\hookrightarrow Q
be a regular immersion of codimension
c
in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
. By
[
9
, Definition 1.1.2]
and
[
14
, Exposé XVI, Définition 2.3.1]
, there is a cycle class
{\rm Cl}_{\eta}\in H_{P}^{2c}(Q,\Lambda(c))
which refines the

c
-th Chern class
c_{c}(\mathcal{N}_{P/Q}^{\vee})\in H^{2c}(P,\Lambda(c))
, where
\mathcal{N}_{P/Q}
is the conormal sheaf associated to the closed immersion
\eta
. We view the cycle class
{\rm Cl}_{\eta}
as a morphism
{\rm Cl}_{\eta}:\Lambda\to\eta^{!}\Lambda(c)[2c]
.

2.9.

Consider a cartesian diagram in
{\rm Sch}_{S}

where
\delta:W\to T
is a regular immersion of codimension
r
.
For any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
, the map
c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\to\mathcal{F}
induces a morphism

(2.9.5)

\displaystyle\delta^{!}:H^{0}(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda)\to H^{0}(X,\mathcal{F}),

which is also induced by
\delta^{!}\xrightarrow{\eqref{delta!ast}}\delta^{\ast}
.
Now assume that
{\rm Cl}_{\delta}:\Lambda\rightarrow\delta^{!}\Lambda(r)[2r]
is an isomorphism.
We view the top Chern class
c_{r}(p^{*}\mathcal{N}_{W/T}^{\vee})
as
an element of
H^{2r}(X,\Lambda(r))
(cf.
[
14
, Exposé XVI, Théorème 1.3]
).
The cup product with this element induces a map

(2.9.6)

\displaystyle H^{0}(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{c_{r}(p^{*}\mathcal{N}_{W/T}^{\vee})}H^{0}(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda(r)[2r])\xrightarrow[\simeq]{id\otimes p^{\ast}{\rm Cl}_{\delta}^{-1}}H^{0}(X,\mathcal{F}),

which is again denoted by
c_{r}(p^{*}\mathcal{N}_{W/T}^{\vee})
.

Lemma 2.10
.

Consider the cartesian diagram (
2.9
).

(1)

We have a commutative diagram

(2)

Assume that
{\rm Cl}_{\delta}:\Lambda\rightarrow\delta^{!}\Lambda(r)[2r]
is an isomorphism. Then the two maps (
2.9.5
) and (
2.9.6
) are the same, i.e.,

(2.10.5)

\displaystyle\delta^{!}=c_{r}(p^{*}\mathcal{N}_{W/T}^{\vee}):H^{0}(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda)\to H^{0}(X,\mathcal{F}).

Proof.

(1)
Note that the excess normal sheaf of the cartesian diagram (
2.9
)
equals to
p^{*}\mathcal{N}_{W/T}^{\vee}
.
By
[
14
, Exposé XVI, Proposition 2.3.2]
, the image of
{\rm Cl}_{\delta}:\Lambda\to\delta^{!}\Lambda(r)[2r]
under the map

(2.10.6)

\displaystyle H^{2r}_{W}(T,\Lambda(r))\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}}H^{2r}_{X}(X,\Lambda(r))=H^{2r}(X,\Lambda(r))

equals to
c_{r}(p^{*}\mathcal{N}_{W/T}^{\vee})\in H^{2r}(X,\Lambda(r))
. By definition of (
2.10.6
),
the class
c_{r}(p^{*}\mathcal{N}_{W/T}^{\vee})
equals to the composition

(2.10.7)

\displaystyle\Lambda=p^{\ast}\Lambda\xrightarrow[\simeq]{p^{\ast}{\rm Cl}_{\delta}}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda(r)[2r]\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:pullbackforanyGdefTrans}}f^{\ast}\Lambda(r)[2r]=\Lambda(r)[2r].

This proves the commutativity of (
(1)
).

(2) For any
\alpha\in H^{0}(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda)
, we write it as
\alpha:\Lambda\to\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda
. The image of
\alpha
under the map (
2.9.6
) equals to

(2.10.8)

\displaystyle\Lambda\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathcal{F}\otimes p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{id\otimes c_{r}(p^{*}\mathcal{N}_{W/T}^{\vee})}\mathcal{F}\otimes p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda(r)[2r]\xrightarrow{id\otimes p^{\ast}{\rm Cl}_{\delta}^{-1}}\mathcal{F}.

By definition, the class
\delta^{!}(\alpha)
is the composition
\Lambda\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{id\otimes\eqref{eq:pullbackforanyGdefTrans}}\mathcal{F}
.
We need to show the following diagram

2.11.

Consider the notation in
2.1
.
If
\delta
is a closed immersion, we can imitate the constructions of
[
1
, 5.1]
and
[
27
, Lemma 3.5]
to define a functor

\delta^{\Delta}:D_{\rm ctf}(Y,\Lambda)\to D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that for any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(Y,\Lambda)
, we have a distinguished triangle

(2.11.1)

\displaystyle i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F}}}i^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{+1}.

Indeed, let
j:T\setminus W\to T
be the open immersion and we put

(2.11.2)

\displaystyle\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{F}:=i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}j_{\ast}\Lambda).

c_{\delta,f,\mathcal{F},-}

to
\delta_{!}\delta^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{\rm adj}\Lambda
, we get a commutative diagram

is an isomorphism since it is the composition of isomorphisms:

Applying
i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}-)
to
\delta_{!}\delta^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{\rm adj}\Lambda\to j_{\ast}\Lambda\xrightarrow{+1}
, we get
the following distinguished triangle

(2.11.9)

\displaystyle i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\delta_{!}\delta^{!}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\rm adj}i^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{+1}.

Then the required distinguished triangle (
2.11.1
) follows from (
2.11
) and (
2.11.9
).

Consider the cartesian diagram (
(2)
). There is a canonical morphism

(2.11.10)

\displaystyle\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{F}=i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}j_{\ast}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\rm b.c}i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}j^{\prime}_{\ast}f_{V}^{\ast}\Lambda)=i^{\Delta}\mathcal{F},

which induces a commutative diagram

For a general
\delta
, one could use
\infty
-enhancement of the étale cohomology theory and define
\delta^{\Delta}
to be the cofiber of
i^{\ast}(-)\otimes^{L}p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\to i^{!}(-)
. Then
\delta^{\Delta}:D_{\rm ctf}(Y,\Lambda)\to D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
is a functor fitting in the distinguished triangle (
2.11.1
). In this paper, we essentially only need the case where
\delta
is a closed immersion.

Lemma 2.12
.

Consider the cartesian diagram (
2.1
).
For any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(Y,\Lambda)
, the morphism
\delta
is
\mathcal{F}
-transversal if and only if the complex
\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{F}
is acyclic on
X
.

Proof.

It follows from the definition of
\mathcal{F}
-transversality and
the distinguished triangle (
2.11.1
).
∎

3.
Cohomological characteristic class

3.1.

In this section,
we summarize the construction and functorial properties of relative cohomological characteristic classes.
Consider the following cartesian diagram in
{\rm Sch}_{S}

where
{\rm pr}_{1}
and
{\rm pr}_{2}
are the projections.
For any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm ctf}(Y,\Lambda)
,
we have canonical morphisms

(3.1.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}={\rm pr}^{\ast}_{1}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}{\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}g^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{c_{g,h,\mathcal{F}}}{\rm pr}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F},

(3.1.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{Y/S}(\mathcal{G})\rightarrow R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}Y}({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F}),

where (
3.1.6
) is adjoint to

(3.1.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}(D_{Y/S}(\mathcal{G})\otimes^{L}\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{id\boxtimes{\rm ev}}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:cForFBoxK}}{\rm pr}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}.

Note that (
3.1.5
) is also a special case of (
3.1.6
) by taking
\mathcal{G}=\Lambda
.
If moreover
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)
, then
(
3.1.6
) is an isomorphism by
[
18
, Proposition 2.5]
(see also
[
30
, Corollary 3.1.5]
).

Recall that a
correspondence
over
S
is a pair of morphisms
X\xleftarrow{c_{1}}C\xrightarrow{c_{2}}Y
over
S
(or equivalently, a morphism
c=(c_{1},c_{2}):C\to X\times_{S}Y
).
For such a
c
, we have a canonical isomorphism by
[
6
, Corollaire 3.1.12.2]

(3.1.8)

\displaystyle R\mathcal{H}om(c^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},c^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{\simeq}c^{!}R\mathcal{H}om({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F}),

which is adjoint to the composition (cf.
[
1
, 1.5]
)

(3.1.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G}\otimes^{L}c_{!}R\mathcal{H}om(c^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},c^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})&\rightarrow c_{!}(c^{\ast}{\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{G}\otimes^{L}R\mathcal{H}om(c^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},c^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F}))\\
&\simeq c_{!}(c_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{G}\otimes^{L}R\mathcal{H}om(c^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},c^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F}))\\
&\xrightarrow{\rm ev}c_{!}c^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F}\simeq c_{!}c^{!}{\rm pr}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\rm adj}{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F}.\end{split}

Now we take
X=Y
and define
P
to be the pull-back of
C
along the diagonal map
\delta\colon X\to X\times_{S}X
:

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.61177pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{c}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.61177pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.71735pt\raise-25.90276pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\times_{S}X.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.1809pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.60417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Box}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

For any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
,
we have canonical isomorphisms

(3.1.14)

\displaystyle c^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{G}))\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{eq:Kunethiso}}c^{!}R\mathcal{H}om({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})\xleftarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{eq:keyisoCshrinkRHOM}}R\mathcal{H}om(c^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},c^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F}).

Combining the inverse of (
3.1.14
) with the canonical map

(3.1.15)

c^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\xrightarrow{{\rm id}\to\delta_{*}\delta^{*}}c^{!}\delta_{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\xrightarrow{{\rm ev}}c^{!}\delta_{*}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\rm b.c}i_{*}\mathcal{K}_{P/S},

we obtain

(3.1.16)

\displaystyle R\mathcal{H}om(c_{2}^{*}\mathcal{F},c_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F})\to i_{*}\mathcal{K}_{P/S},

(3.1.17)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}:{\rm Hom}(c_{2}^{*}\mathcal{F},c_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F})\to{\rm Hom}(\Lambda_{C},i_{*}\mathcal{K}_{P/S})=H^{0}(P,\mathcal{K}_{P/S}).

In particular, taking the special correspondence
C=X
and
c=\delta
, we have canonical morphisms

(3.1.18)

\displaystyle R\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})\to\mathcal{K}_{X/S},

(3.1.19)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}:{\rm Hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})\to H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S}).

The
relative (cohomological) characteristic class
of
\mathcal{F}
is defined to be (cf.
[
30
, Definition 3.6]
and
[
18
, 2.20]
)

(3.1.20)

\displaystyle C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})\coloneq{\rm Tr}({\rm id}_{\mathcal{F}})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S}).

The map
\mathcal{F}\mapsto C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})
defines a group homomorphism

(3.1.21)

\displaystyle C_{X/S}:K_{0}(X/S,\Lambda)\to H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S}).

Note that if
S
is the spectrum of a field
k
, then
C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})
is the characteristic class introduced by Abbes and Saito (cf.
[
1
, Definition 2.1.1]
).
The following lemma computes the characteristic classes of locally constant sheaves.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
X
be a connected scheme in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
and
\mathcal{L}
a locally constant constructible flat sheaf of
\Lambda
-modules on
X
. Then for any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)
, we have

(3.2.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L})={\rm rank}\mathcal{L}\cdot C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}).

In particular,
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{L})={\rm rank}\mathcal{L}\cdot C_{X/S}(\Lambda)
.

Proof.

In the following, we put
\mathcal{L}^{\vee}=R\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{L},\Lambda)=D_{X/X}(\mathcal{L})
. By definition,
the class
C_{X/X}(\mathcal{L})={\rm rank}\mathcal{L}\in H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/X})=\Lambda
is the composition

(3.2.2)

\displaystyle\Lambda\xrightarrow{1_{\mathcal{L}}}R\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L})\simeq\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{{\rm ev}}\Lambda,

where
{\rm ev}
is the evaluation map.
We have a commutative diagram

\displaystyle\small\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.31248pt\hbox{\kern 6.21107pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.21107pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.36249pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.11383pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.8158pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 48.21107pt\raise-32.8158pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 179.15248pt\raise-32.8158pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 283.22562pt\raise-27.36581pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.57501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm ev}\otimes{\rm ev}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 318.75815pt\raise-32.8158pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 318.75815pt\raise-32.8158pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\otimes\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.21107pt\raise-65.63159pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.00618pt\raise-60.07658pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.505pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{1_{\mathcal{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.08873pt\raise-65.63159pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.08873pt\raise-65.63159pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.32066pt\raise-49.28618pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.6625pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{L}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.32066pt\raise-40.37828pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 112.91917pt\raise-58.54686pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.85973pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\eqref{eq:keyisoKu}\&\eqref{eq:evaMap}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 196.03012pt\raise-65.63159pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 196.03012pt\raise-65.63159pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 226.95184pt\raise-49.07202pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.6625pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{L}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 226.95184pt\raise-41.32826pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 264.19365pt\raise-59.1041pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.1975pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm ev}\otimes C_{X/X}(\mathcal{L})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 317.47343pt\raise-65.63159pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 317.47343pt\raise-65.63159pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\otimes\Lambda.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

The composition of the first row is
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L})
, while the bottom row gives
{\rm rank}\mathcal{L}\cdot C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})
. Thus
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L})={\rm rank}\mathcal{L}\cdot C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})
.
∎

We could also define relative characteristic classes in a more general case that only assumes local acyclicity away from small closed subsets. We first note the following lemma.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
X\in{\rm Sch}_{S}
and
Z\subseteq X
a closed subscheme. Let
U=X\setminus Z
.
Assume that
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{S}})=0
and
H^{1}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{S}})=0
.
Then we have

(3.3.1)

\displaystyle H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})\simeq H^{0}(U,\mathcal{K}_{U/S}).

Proof.

Let
\tau:Z\hookrightarrow X
be the closed immersion and
j:U\hookrightarrow X
the open immersion. The assertion follows from the following long exact sequence

(3.3.2)

\displaystyle H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/S})\to H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})\to H^{0}(U,\mathcal{K}_{U/S})\to H^{1}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/S}),

which is induced from the distinguished triangle
\tau_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{Z/S}=\tau_{\ast}\tau^{!}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\to\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\to j_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{U/S}\xrightarrow{+1}
.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

Assume that
X
and
S
are smooth schemes over a field and that
X\to S
is of relative dimension
r
.
If
Z\hookrightarrow X
is a closed immersion of codimension
>r
, then we have
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{S}})=0
and
H^{1}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{S}})=0
by semi-purity (cf.
[
9
, §8]
).

Definition 3.5
.

Under the assumptions in Lemma
3.3
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that
\mathcal{F}|_{U}\in D_{\rm ctf}(U/S,\Lambda)
.
We define the relative (cohomological) characteristic class of
\mathcal{F}
to be

(3.5.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})\coloneqq C_{U/S}(\mathcal{F}|_{U})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S}).

3.6.

For any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
,
we call elements of
{\rm Hom}(c_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G},c_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F})

cohomological correspondences
from
\mathcal{G}
to
\mathcal{F}
. By the isomorphism (
3.1.14
), a cohomological correspondence
u:c_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G}\to c_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}
is equivalent to a map
\Lambda\to c^{!}R\mathcal{H}om({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})
(resp.
\Lambda\to c^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))
). By adjunction, this is also equivalent to a map

c_{!}\Lambda\to R\mathcal{H}om({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{G},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})
(resp.
c_{!}\Lambda\to\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})
).
In the following, we will identify these descriptions freely.

3.7.

We give an equivalent description of the relative (cohomological) characteristic classes.
Let
X\in{\rm Sch}_{S}
and
\delta:X\to X\times_{S}X
the diagonal morphism.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)
.
The canonical isomorphism

R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}X}({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})\xleftarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})

induces an isomorphism

\delta^{\ast}R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}X}({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})\xleftarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}).

Thus the evaluation map
\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})\to\mathcal{K}_{X/S}
induces a map

(3.7.1)

\displaystyle v_{\mathcal{F}}:\delta^{\ast}R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}X}({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})\to\mathcal{K}_{X/S}.

Let
u:\delta_{!}\Lambda\to R\mathcal{H}om({\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})
be a cohomological
correspondence. Then

{\rm Tr}(u)\in H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})
equals the cohomology class
of the following composition

(3.7.2)

\displaystyle\Lambda=\delta^{\ast}\delta_{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{\delta^{\ast}(u)}\delta^{\ast}R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}X}({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{v_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}.

We also write the class
{\rm Tr}(u)
in the form

(3.7.3)

\displaystyle\delta_{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{u}R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}X}({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{v_{\mathcal{F}}}\delta_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}.

Taking
u={\rm id}_{\mathcal{F}}
to be the
cohomological correspondence defined by the identity of
\mathcal{F}
, we get that
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})={\rm Tr}({\rm id}_{\mathcal{F}})
equals the cohomology class of the composition

(3.7.4)

\displaystyle\delta_{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{{\rm id}_{\mathcal{F}}}R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}X}({\rm pr}^{*}_{2}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{v_{\mathcal{F}}}\delta_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}.

3.8.

We first review the pull-back property of the relative characteristic classes.
Consider a cartesian diagram of schemes

33.00693pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 33.00693pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 17.1111pt\raise-16.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.60417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Box}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where
f
is a separated morphism of finite type, and
b:S^{\prime}\to S
is a morphism between Noetherian schemes.
For any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)
, the object
b_{X}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}
on
X^{\prime}
is also universally locally acyclic over
S^{\prime}
.
Thus we have a well-defined functor

(3.8.5)

\displaystyle b_{X}^{*}\colon

\displaystyle D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)\to D_{\rm ctf}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},\Lambda),

and a well-defined map

(3.8.6)

\displaystyle b_{X}^{*}\colon

\displaystyle K_{0}(X/S,\Lambda)\to K_{0}(X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},\Lambda).

Note that the following base change morphism

(3.8.7)

b_{X}^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}=b_{X}^{\ast}f^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{\rm b.c}f^{\prime!}b^{\ast}\Lambda=\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}

induces a pull-back morphism on cohomology groups

(3.8.8)

b_{X}^{*}\colon H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})\to H^{0}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}).

.

),

In a previous version of this paper, Proposition
3.9
is proved by verifying the commutativity of certain diagrams. Weizhe Zheng remarked that the categorical method in
[
18
]
could also be used here in an email to the authors and Tomoyuki Abe. We refer to
[
3
, 1.3]
for that proof.

Proof.

Here we give a sketched proof.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=b_{X}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}
.
We put

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{S}

\displaystyle=R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}X}({\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}),\;\;\qquad\mathcal{T}_{S}=\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{S^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=R\mathcal{H}om_{X^{\prime}\times_{S^{\prime}}X^{\prime}}({\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}^{\prime},{\rm pr}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}),\quad\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes^{L}_{S^{\prime}}D_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}).

Consider the following cartesian diagram

180.90707pt\raise-7.85115pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 224.88245pt\raise-26.06184pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b_{X}^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 247.22485pt\raise-14.10928pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eqref{eq:3pullbackK}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 247.22485pt\raise-9.60115pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where
(a)
is given by

b_{X}^{\ast}\delta^{!}\mathcal{H}_{S}\xrightarrow[\eqref{eq:pullbackforanyGdefTrans}]{\rm b.c}\delta^{\prime!}(b_{X}\times b_{X})^{\ast}\mathcal{H}_{S}\xrightarrow{\rm b.c}\delta^{\prime!}\mathcal{H}_{S^{\prime}}
,
the morphism
(b)
is given by

b_{X}^{\ast}\delta^{!}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow[\eqref{eq:pullbackforanyGdefTrans}]{\rm b.c}\delta^{\prime!}(b_{X}\times b_{X})^{\ast}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{\rm b.c}\delta^{\prime!}\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}
,
and
(c)
is given by
b_{X}^{\ast}\delta^{\ast}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\delta^{\prime\ast}(b_{X}\times b_{X})^{\ast}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{\rm b.c}\delta^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{T}_{S^{\prime}}
.
By the above commutative diagram (
3.8
), we get
b_{X}^{\ast}C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})=C_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(b_{X}^{\ast}\mathcal{F})
.
∎

3.10.

Now we recall the proper push-forward property of the relative characteristic classes.
Let
s:X\to X^{\prime}
be a proper morphism in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
.
For any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)
, then

s_{\ast}\mathcal{F}
is universally locally acyclic over
S
by
[
18
, Theorem 2.16 and Proposition 2.23]
.
Thus we have a well-defined functor

(3.10.1)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}\colon D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)\to D_{\rm ctf}(X^{\prime}/S,\Lambda),

and a well-defined map

(3.10.2)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}\colon K_{0}(X/S,\Lambda)\to K_{0}(X^{\prime}/S,\Lambda).

Proposition 3.11
.

If
s\colon X\to X^{\prime}
is a proper morphism in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
, then
the following diagram commutes

where
(a)
is given by

(3.11.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}s_{\ast}\delta^{\ast}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))&\simeq\alpha_{\ast}\delta_{0\ast}\delta_{0}^{\ast}\beta^{\ast}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\xrightarrow{\rm adj}\alpha_{\ast}\beta^{\ast}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\\
&\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\rm proper~b.c}\delta^{\prime\ast}(s\times s)_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\to\delta^{\prime\ast}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})).\end{split}

By the commutativity of (
3.10
) and an equivalent description (
3.7.4
) of the characteristic classes, we get
s_{\ast}C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})=C_{X^{\prime}/S}(s_{\ast}\mathcal{F})
.
∎

3.12.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
1.9
under the condition
Z=\emptyset
.
Consider a commutative diagram in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
:

which induces a homomorphism

(3.12.9)

\displaystyle\delta^{!}:H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y})\xrightarrow{}H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S}).

When

is a smooth morphism, for any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/Y,\Lambda)
,
we have
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/S,\Lambda)

by

, Appendice to Th.finitude, Corollaire 2.7]
. In this case, there is a canonical homomorphism

(3.12.10)

\displaystyle K_{0}(X/Y,\Lambda)\to K_{0}(X/S,\Lambda);\quad\mathcal{F}\mapsto\mathcal{F}.

If
X=Y
is smooth over
S
and connected, then any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X/X,\Lambda)
has locally constant cohomology sheaves and
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})={\rm rank}\mathcal{F}\cdot c_{r}(\Omega_{X/S}^{1,\vee})
.

Proof.

First note that for any closed immersion
d
, there is a canonical morphism
d^{!}\to d^{\ast}
defined by

d^{!}\simeq d^{\ast}d_{!}d^{!}\xrightarrow{\rm adj}d^{\ast}
.
Consider the commutative diagram (
2.7
).
In the following, we put

(3.13.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{S}=R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}X}({\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{T}_{S}=\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}),

(3.13.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{Y}=R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{Y}X}({\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{T}_{Y}=\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{Y}D_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F}).

For any
\alpha:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
which is given by a morphism

\Lambda\to R\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})=\delta_{0}^{!}\mathcal{H}_{S}
, the trace

C_{X/S}(\alpha):={\rm Tr}(\alpha)\in H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})
is given by
the composition (cf. (
3.1.15
)-(
3.1.20
))

(3.13.6)

\displaystyle\Lambda\xrightarrow{\alpha}\delta_{0}^{!}\mathcal{H}_{S}\xleftarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{eq:Kunethiso}}\delta_{0}^{!}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{}\delta_{0}^{\ast}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{\rm ev}\mathcal{K}_{X/S},

and the trace
C_{X/Y}(\alpha):={\rm Tr}(\alpha)\in H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y})
is given by
the composition

(3.13.7)

\displaystyle\Lambda\xrightarrow{\alpha}\delta_{1}^{!}\mathcal{H}_{Y}\xleftarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{eq:Kunethiso}}\delta_{1}^{!}\mathcal{T}_{Y}\xrightarrow{}\delta_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{T}_{Y}\xrightarrow{\rm ev}\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}.

The morphism
\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:defDistrik1-section3}}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}
induces a map
\mathcal{T}_{Y}\to i^{\ast}\mathcal{T}_{S}
.
There is a commutative diagram

From this we get
C_{X/S}(\alpha)=\delta^{!}(C_{X/Y}(\alpha))
, which shows the commutativity of (
3.13.1
).

If
g:Y\to S
is smooth, the equality (
3.13.2
) is obtained by applying Lemma
2.10
to the cartesian diagram (
2.7
).
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

Consider the following commutative diagrams in
{\rm Sch}_{S}

where
\delta
is the diagonal morphism,
i
is the base change of
\delta
,

p_{i}={\rm pr}_{i}
and
q_{j}={\rm pr}_{j}
are the projections, and
p=f^{\prime}q_{2}
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm{ctf}}(X,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm{ctf}}(X^{\prime},\Lambda)
.
Assume that
\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}=g^{!}\Lambda
is locally constant and that
\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\otimes^{L}\delta^{!}\Lambda\simeq\Lambda

(
cf. (
2.6.5
)
)
.
Then we have a commutative diagram

Proof.

In the following, we identify
\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y}
with
\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S}\otimes^{L}f^{\prime\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda
by using the isomorphism (
2.7.11
).
The composition

\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{Y}D_{X^{\prime}/Y}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:Kunethiso}}R\mathcal{H}om({q}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G},{q}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow{}i^{!}R\mathcal{H}om({p}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G},{p}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})
is adjoint
to the following composition (cf. (
3.1.7
) and (
3.1.9
))

(3.14.11)

\displaystyle i_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{Y}D_{X^{\prime}/Y}(\mathcal{G})\otimes^{L}{q}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\rm ev}i_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{Y}\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y})\xrightarrow{i_{!}c_{f^{\prime},f,\mathcal{F}}}i_{!}{q}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}\simeq i_{!}i^{!}{p}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\rm adj}{p}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F},

and

\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{Y}D_{X^{\prime}/Y}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{c_{\delta,f\times f^{\prime}}}i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{G}))\xrightarrow{}i^{!}R\mathcal{H}om({p}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{G},{p}^{!}_{1}\mathcal{F})
is adjoint to

(3.14.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}i_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{Y}D_{X^{\prime}/Y}(\mathcal{G})\otimes{q}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{c_{\delta,f\times f^{\prime}}}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{G})\otimes{q}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\rm ev}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S}\xrightarrow{c_{h^{\prime},h,\mathcal{F}}}{p}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}.\end{split}

Now the result follows from the following commutative diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 81.93292pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-52.90532pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{G})\otimes^{L}{p}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.21535pt\raise-28.48308pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm ev}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.43341pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 101.43341pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 173.0397pt\raise-25.35335pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.85335pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i^{!}c_{h^{\prime},h,\mathcal{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 211.75511pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 211.75511pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{i^{!}{p}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F},}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where the left diagram is commutative by applying
c_{\delta,f\times f^{\prime},-}
to the map
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{G})\xrightarrow{\rm ev}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y}
, the right diagram is commutative since the two compositions are both adjoint to

(3.14.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}q_{1!}(q_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}q_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y})&\xrightarrow{\rm proj.formula}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}q_{1!}q_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y}=\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}q_{1!}q_{2}^{\ast}f^{\prime!}\Lambda\\
&\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:pullbackforanyGdefTrans}}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}q_{1!}q_{1}^{!}f^{\ast}\Lambda\xrightarrow{\rm adj}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\Lambda=\mathcal{F}.\end{split}

∎

For later convenience, we note the following blow-up formula for characteristic classes.

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
X
and
Y
be smooth connected schemes over a perfect field
k
and let
i\colon Y\hookrightarrow X
be a closed immersion of codimension
r\geq 1
.
Let
\pi\colon\widetilde{X}\to X
be the blow-up of
X
along
Y
and
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
.
Then we have:

(3.15.1)

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}(C_{\widetilde{X}/k}(\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}))

\displaystyle=C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})+(r-1)\cdot i_{\ast}(C_{Y/k}(i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}))\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}),

(3.15.2)

\displaystyle\pi_{\ast}(cc_{\widetilde{X}}(\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}))

\displaystyle=cc_{X}(\mathcal{F})+(r-1)\cdot i_{\ast}(cc_{Y}(i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}))\qquad{\rm in}\quad CH_{0}(X),

where
cc_{X}:=cc_{X,0}:K_{0}(X,\Lambda)\to{\rm CH}_{0}(X)
is the morphism defined in
[
22
, Definition 6.7.2]
via the characteristic cycles of constructible étale sheaves.

In this paper, we only need the case where
r=2
.

Proof.

The equality (
3.15.2
) follows from
[
28
, Lemma 3.3.2 and Remark 3.3.3]
. We prove (
3.15.1
).
By
[
14
, Exposé XVI, Proposition 2.2.2.1]
, we have an isomorphism on
X
:

(3.15.3)

R\pi_{\ast}\Lambda\simeq\Lambda\oplus\bigoplus_{t=1}^{r-1}(i_{\ast}\Lambda)(-t)[-2t].

By the projection formula, we have

(3.15.4)

R\pi_{\ast}\Lambda\otimes^{L}\mathcal{F}\simeq R\pi_{\ast}(\Lambda\otimes^{L}\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F})=R\pi_{\ast}\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\qquad{\rm and}\qquad i_{\ast}\Lambda\otimes^{L}\mathcal{F}\simeq i_{\ast}i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}.

By (
3.15.3
) and (
3.15.4
), we get

(3.15.5)

R\pi_{\ast}\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathcal{F}\oplus\bigoplus_{t=1}^{r-1}(i_{\ast}i^{\ast}\mathcal{F})(-t)[-2t].

Now the required formula (
3.15.1
) follows from Lemma
3.2
, Proposition
3.11
and (
3.15.5
).
∎

3.16.

Now we recall that the cohomological characteristic class is compatible with the specialization map.
Before that, let us recall the nearby cycles functor over a general base.
Let
h:X\to S
be a morphism of finite type between Noetherian schemes.
Let
s\leftarrow t
be a specialization map of geometric points of
S
, i.e.,
a geometric point
t\to S_{(s)}
of the strict henselization of
S
at
s
.
Consider the cartesian
diagram

Then we define

(3.16.5)

\displaystyle\Psi_{h,s\leftarrow t}:=i^{\ast}Rj_{\ast}:D^{+}(X_{t},\Lambda)\to D^{+}(X_{s},\Lambda).

In this paper, we denote
\Psi_{h,s\leftarrow t}
by
\Psi_{h}
for simplicity.
When
S_{(s)}
is a trait, then
\Psi_{h,s\leftarrow t}
is the classical nearby cycles functor (cf.
[
8
, Th.finitude, 3.1]
).

3.17.

Assume that
S
is a henselian trait with the generic point
\eta
. Let
s
be a geometric point of
S
above its closed point.
Consider a commutative diagram in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
:

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 55.36064pt\raise-21.35616pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.24303pt\raise-26.0837pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.81247pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

with
g
smooth. We denote again by
\Psi_{h}
the composition
D^{+}(X_{\eta},\Lambda)\to D^{+}(X_{\overline{\eta}},\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\Psi_{h,s\leftarrow\overline{\eta}}}D^{+}(X_{s},\Lambda)
.
Since
g
is smooth, we have
\Psi_{g}(\Lambda_{Y_{\eta}})\simeq\Lambda_{Y_{s}}
.
There is a canonical morphism

(3.17.5)

\displaystyle\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}})\to\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}}

given by the composition
\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}})=\Psi_{h}(f_{\eta}^{!}\Lambda)\xrightarrow{\rm b.c}f_{s}^{!}\Psi_{g}(\Lambda)\simeq f_{s}^{!}\Lambda=\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}}
. The specialization map

(3.17.6)

\displaystyle{\rm sp}={\rm sp}_{s}:H^{0}(X_{\eta},\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}})\to H^{0}(X_{s},\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}})

is induced by the following composition

(3.17.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}RHom(\Lambda,\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}})&\xrightarrow{\Psi_{h}}RHom(\Psi_{h}(\Lambda),\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}}))\\
&\xrightarrow{\Lambda\to\Psi_{h}(\Lambda)}RHom(\Lambda,\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}}))\xrightarrow{\eqref{PsiKXYdef}}RHom(\Lambda,\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}}).\end{split}

When
g={\rm id}
, we have the following specialization formula for characteristic classes.

Proposition 3.18

(cf.
[
18
, Corollary 3.10]
and
[
29
, Proposition 1.3.5]
)
.

Let
S
be a henselian trait with the generic point
\eta
.
Let
s
be a geometric point of
S
above its closed point.
Let
h:X\to S
be a separated morphism of finite type and
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X_{\eta},\Lambda)
. Then we have

(3.18.1)

\displaystyle{\rm sp}(C_{X_{\eta}/\eta}(\mathcal{F}))=C_{X_{s}/s}(\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{F}))\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X_{s},\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/{s}}),

where
{\rm sp}:H^{0}(X_{\eta},\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/\eta})\to H^{0}(X_{s},\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/{s}})
is the specialization map.

In particular, if
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that
h
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
, then
\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{\eta}})\simeq\mathcal{F}|_{X_{s}}
and hence

(3.18.2)

\displaystyle{\rm sp}(C_{X_{\eta}/{\eta}}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{\eta}}))=C_{X_{s}/s}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{s}})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X_{s},\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/{s}}).

Remark 3.19
.

Consider the diagram (
3.17
) with
g
smooth.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X_{\eta},\Lambda)
such that
f_{\eta}
(resp.
f_{s}
) is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
(resp.
\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{F})
). Then one may prove the following formula

(3.19.1)

\displaystyle{\rm sp}(C_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}}(\mathcal{F}))=C_{X_{s}/Y_{s}}(\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{F}))\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X_{s},\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}}).

4.
Non-acyclicity class

4.1.

In this section, we construct the non-acyclicity classes supported on the non-acyclicity locus.
Consider a commutative diagram in
{\rm Sch}_{S}
:

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.77083pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.77083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.77084pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.9269pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.77083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 35.30554pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 69.84026pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 111.11801pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where
\tau:Z\to X
is a closed immersion.
Let
\delta:Y\to Y\times_{S}Y
be the diagonal morphism.
Consider the following conditions:

(C1)

\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}=g^{!}\Lambda
is locally constant and that
\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\otimes^{L}\delta^{!}\Lambda\simeq\Lambda
(cf. (
2.6.5
)).
For example, this condition holds if
g
is a smooth morphism.

(C2)

The closed subscheme
Z\subseteq X
satisfies
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{Y}})=0
and
H^{1}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{Y}})=0
.

(C3)

Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that

X\setminus Z\to Y
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
and that
h:X\to S
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
.

Let us clarify our strategy to define the non-acyclicity classes in the following:

•

Using (C1), we have a distinguished triangle

\mathcal{K}_{X/{Y}}\to\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\to\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}\xrightarrow{+1}
(cf. (
4.2.13
)).

•

Under (C1) and (C3), we construct the non-acyclicity class
\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\in H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/{S}})
.

•

When (C2) holds, we show that

H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{S}})=H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}})\to H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/{S}})
is an isomorphism. Thus under the three conditions (C1)-(C3), the class
\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
defines the cohomology class
C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\in H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})
.

In the rest of this section, we always assume that the conditions (C1) and (C3) are satisfied.

4.2.

Let
i:X\times_{Y}X\to X\times_{S}X
be the base change of the diagonal morphism
\delta:Y\to Y\times_{S}Y
:

where
\delta_{0}
and
\delta_{1}
are the diagonal morphisms.
Applying (
2.11.1
) to
\mathcal{K}_{X/S}
and
\delta_{0\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}
, we get an isomorphism between distinguished triangles
