where the commutativity follows from (
(2)
).
We put

(4.2.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}:=\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\simeq\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}\delta_{0\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}}.

By Proposition
2.7
.(2), we have an isomorphism

(4.2.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}&\xleftarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda.\end{split}

Then we rewrite the first row of (
4.2
) as the following distinguished triangle

(4.2.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{X/{Y}}\to\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\to\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}\xrightarrow{+1}.

It induces an exact sequence

(4.2.14)

\displaystyle H_{Z}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/{Y}})\to H_{Z}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}})\to H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})\to H^{1}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/{Y}}).

If the condition (C2) in
4.1
holds, then the map

(4.2.15)

\displaystyle H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/{S}})=H_{Z}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})

is an isomorphism.

Remark 4.3
.

By (
2.11.2
),
\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}\simeq\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\otimes^{L}\delta_{0}^{\ast}(f\times f)^{\ast}j_{Y\ast}\Lambda
,
where
j_{Y}:Y\times_{S}Y\setminus\delta(Y)\to Y\times_{S}Y
is the open immersion.

4.4.

Since
i^{\ast}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\otimes p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\simeq(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{Y}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\otimes p^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\overset{\eqref{lem:eq:Gysim2}}{\simeq}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{Y}D_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})
,
we have the following distinguished triangles by applying (
2.11.1
)
to
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})
:

(4.4.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{Y}D_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\to i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\to\delta^{\Delta}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\xrightarrow{+1},

(4.4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}D_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\to\delta_{1}^{\ast}i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\to\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\xrightarrow{+1}.

Since
X\setminus Z\to Y
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
, the morphism
\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}D_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})\to\delta_{1}^{\ast}i^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))
is an isomorphism on
X\setminus Z
by Proposition
2.7
.(3).
Thus
\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/{S}}(\mathcal{F}))

is supported on
Z
.

4.5.

Recall that the relative characteristic class
C_{X/{S}}(\mathcal{F})

is defined by the composition (cf. (
3.7.4
))

(4.5.1)

\displaystyle\delta_{0\ast}\Lambda=\delta_{0!}\Lambda\to\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})\to\delta_{0\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}}.

It induces the following morphisms

(4.5.2)

\displaystyle\Lambda=\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta_{1\ast}\Lambda\overset{\rm b.c}{\simeq}\delta_{1}^{\ast}i^{!}\delta_{0\ast}\Lambda\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:Dchf}}\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}\delta_{0\ast}\Lambda\to\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\to\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}\delta_{0\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}}.

Note that
\tau:Z\hookrightarrow X
denotes the closed immersion.
Since the complex
\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\simeq\tau_{\ast}\tau^{\ast}\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))
is supported on
Z
,
hence (
4.5.2
) defines a cohomology class

(4.5.3)

\displaystyle\tau^{\ast}\Lambda\to\tau^{\ast}\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes_{S}^{L}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}))\to\tau^{!}(\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}\delta_{0\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}}),

which we denote by

(4.5.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\in H_{Z}^{0}(X,\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}\delta_{0\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}})=H_{Z}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}).

If the condition (C2) in
4.1
holds, then

H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}})\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:isomOnHZassC2}}H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/{S}})
is an isomorphism. In this case, the class

\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\in H_{Z}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/{S}})

defines an element of
H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/{S}})
, which is denoted by

C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
.

Definition 4.6
.

Under the notation and conditions (C1) and (C3) in
4.1
.
We define the

non-acyclicity class
of
\mathcal{F}
to be the class

\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\in H_{Z}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/{S}})
.
If moreover the condition (C2) holds, we also call

C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})=\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\in H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})
the
non-acyclicity class
of
\mathcal{F}
.

Remark 4.7
.

Under the conditions (C1) and (C3) in
4.1
.
If
Z^{\prime}\subseteq X
is another closed subscheme such that
Z\subseteq Z^{\prime}
, then
\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})
equals the image of

\widetilde{C}^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})
under the canonical map
H_{Z}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/{S}})\to H_{Z^{\prime}}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/{S}})
.

4.8.

Now we turn to study functorial properties of the non-acyclicity classes. We first consider the pull-back property.
Consider the notation in
4.1
and assume the conditions (C1) and (C3) hold.
Let
b:S^{\prime}\to S
be a morphism of Noetherian schemes. Let
U=X\setminus Z
. We denote by
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}
the base change of
X
. Similar to define
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
,
Y^{\prime}
,
Z^{\prime}
,
U^{\prime}
,
f^{\prime}
,
g^{\prime}
,
h^{\prime}
and
\tau^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
. Let
b_{X}
and
b_{Z}
be the base change of
b
by
X\to S
and
Z\to S
respectively.
We form the following commutative diagrams

, the base change morphism (

) induces a canonical morphism

\displaystyle b_{X}^{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}\to\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}.

4.9.

In the following, we always assume that the following condition
(C^{\prime}1)
holds:

(
C^{\prime}1
)

\mathcal{K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}=g^{\prime!}\Lambda
is locally constant and that
\mathcal{K}_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}\delta^{\prime!}\Lambda\simeq\Lambda
.

Then the class
\widetilde{C}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{Z^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
is well-defined.
If moreover the following condition
(C^{\prime}2)
holds, then the class
C_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{Z^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
is well-defined.

(
C^{\prime}2
)

The closed subscheme
Z^{\prime}\subseteq X^{\prime}
satisfies
H^{0}(Z^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{Z^{\prime}/{Y^{\prime}}})=0
and
H^{1}(Z^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{Z^{\prime}/{Y^{\prime}}})=0
.

\delta_{1}^{\ast}i^{\ast}(-)\otimes^{L}f^{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\to\delta_{1}^{\ast}i^{!}(-)\to\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}(-)\xrightarrow{+1}
to (
4.5.1
), we get a commutative diagram

Consider the pull-back morphisms

b_{X}^{\ast}:H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})\to H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\quad{\rm and}\quad b_{X}^{\ast}:H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})\to H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}).

The above commutative diagram implies the following result:

Proposition 4.11
.

Under the notation in
4.8
, and the condition
(C^{\prime}1)
in
4.9
, we have

(4.11.1)

\displaystyle b_{X}^{\ast}\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})=\widetilde{C}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{Z^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\qquad{\rm in}\qquad H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}).

If moreover the conditions (C2) and
(C^{\prime}2)
hold, then we have

(4.11.2)

\displaystyle b_{X}^{\ast}C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})=C_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{Z^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\qquad{\rm in}\qquad H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}).

4.12.

Now we study the proper push-forward property of non-acyclicity classes.

4.1
), which is depicted
as the following left commutative diagram in
{\rm Sch}_{S}

where
\tau
,
\tau^{\prime}
,
\tau_{0}
and
\tau_{1}
are closed immersions.
Consider the notation in the diagram (
4.2
). We put the prime symbol
′
on the corresponding diagram for
Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}/Y/S
. Now
the left diagram (
4.12
) induces the right one.
Assume that the conditions (C1) and (C3) in
4.1
hold and that
s:X\to X^{\prime}
is proper.
Then the morphism
X^{\prime}\setminus Z^{\prime}\to Y
is also universally locally acyclic relatively to
s_{\ast}\mathcal{F}|_{X^{\prime}\setminus Z^{\prime}}
by
[
18
, Theorem 2.16 and Proposition 2.23]
. Now the non-acyclicity classes

(4.12.11)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})\in H_{Z}^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})\quad{\rm and}\quad\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}(s_{\ast}\mathcal{F})\in H_{Z^{\prime}}^{0}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S})

are well-defined.

4.13.

We define proper push-forward maps

(4.13.1)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}:H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})\to H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S}),

(4.13.2)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}:H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})\to H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}).

First, we have a morphism

(4.13.3)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}=s_{!}\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}\to\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y}=f^{\prime!}\Lambda,

which is defined to be the adjunction of the composition

f^{\prime}_{!}s_{!}f^{!}\Lambda\simeq f_{!}f^{!}\Lambda\xrightarrow{{\rm adj}}\Lambda
.
Similarly, there is a canonical morphism

(4.13.4)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\to\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S}.

Therefore the composition

(4.13.5)

\displaystyle\tau^{\prime}_{!}s_{Z\ast}\tau^{!}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\simeq s_{\ast}\tau_{!}\tau^{!}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\xrightarrow{\rm adj}s_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:ppForNTclass4}}\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S}

induces a morphism

s_{Z\ast}\tau^{!}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\to\tau^{\prime!}\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S}
,
which gives the required morphism (
4.13.1
). By Remark
4.3
, the morphism (
4.13.3
) induces a canonical morphism

(4.13.6)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}\to\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}.

It further induces the required morphism (
4.13.2
).
Consider the distinguished triangle (
4.2.13
). We obtain a commutative diagram

Proposition 4.14
.

Under the notation and assumptions in
4.12
,
we have

(4.14.1)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}(\widetilde{C}^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}))=\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}(s_{\ast}\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}).

If moreover
H_{Z^{\prime}}^{0}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/{Y^{\prime}}})=H_{Z^{\prime}}^{1}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/{Y^{\prime}}})=0
, then

(4.14.2)

\displaystyle s_{\ast}(\widetilde{C}^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}))=C^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}(s_{\ast}\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S}).

3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{s_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/{S}}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 315.43419pt\raise-48.05771pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 332.8582pt\raise-60.69516pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

This proves the equality (
4.14.1
).
∎

The construction of non-acyclicity classes is étale local in the following sense.

Proposition 4.15
.

Consider a commutative diagram in
{\rm Sch}_{S}

0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 111.60413pt\raise-31.62003pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 98.19899pt\raise-52.93654pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.7569pt\raise-57.77228pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 38.1111pt\raise-62.8978pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.04858pt\raise-62.8978pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 116.17357pt\raise-62.8978pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where
e_{1}
and
e_{0}
are étale morphisms,
Z^{\prime}=e_{1}^{-1}(Z)
,
h^{\prime}=he_{1}
and
g^{\prime}=ge_{0}
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=e_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}
.
Assume that the conditions (C1) and (C3)
in
4.1
hold for
g
and
\mathcal{F}
respectively.
Then we have

(4.15.7)

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\ast}\widetilde{C}^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})=\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\;{\rm in}\;H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S})=H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}),

where
e_{1}^{\ast}:H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S})\to H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S})=H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S})
is the pull-back morphism.
If moreover the condition (C2) in
4.1
holds for
Z/X/Y/S
and
Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}/Y/S
, then we have

(4.15.8)

\displaystyle e_{1}^{\ast}C^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})=C^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=C^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S}),

where
e_{1}^{\ast}:H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})\to H^{0}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime}/S})
is the pull-back morphism.

Proof.

It is clear that the condition (C1) in
4.1
holds for
g^{\prime}
.
Since
e_{1}
is étale, the morphism
h^{\prime}
(resp.
X^{\prime}\setminus Z^{\prime}\to Y
) is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|_{X^{\prime}\setminus Z^{\prime}}
).
Since
e_{0}
is étale, the morphism
X^{\prime}\setminus Z^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
is also universally locally acyclic relatively to

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|_{X^{\prime}\setminus Z^{\prime}}
.
So the condition (C3)
in
4.1
holds for
X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S
and
X^{\prime}/Y/S
. Hence
\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
and
\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
are well-defined.
Now we show
\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
. Consider the following commutative diagram

(4.15.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times_{S}X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}\times_{S}Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times_{S}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times_{Y}X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime}}X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{0}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}\times f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{0}\times e_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1}^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{0}}

where squares and parallelograms are cartesian. Since
e_{0}:Y^{\prime}\to Y
is étale, the diagonal morphism
\gamma_{0}:Y^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}
is an open immersion. So is its base change
\eta^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime}}X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}\times_{Y}X^{\prime}
.
For
X/Y/S
, we have the diagram (
4.10
). Similar for
X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S
and
X^{\prime}/Y/S
.
Since
\gamma_{0}
is an open immersion and that
e_{0}\times e_{0}
is étale,
we have isomorphisms
{(f^{\prime}e_{0})}^{\ast}{\delta}^{!}\Lambda\simeq f^{\prime\ast}e_{0}^{\ast}{\delta}^{!}\Lambda\simeq f^{\prime\ast}\gamma_{0}^{\ast}{\gamma}_{1}^{!}\Lambda\simeq f^{\prime\ast}\delta^{\prime!}\Lambda
and

\delta_{1}^{\prime\ast}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes_{Y^{\prime}}^{L}D_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))\simeq\delta_{1}^{\prime\prime\ast}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes_{Y}^{L}D_{X^{\prime}/Y}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
.
Similarly, one can check that all objects in the diagram (
4.10
) for

X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S
are isomorphic to those for
X^{\prime}/Y/S
. This proves
\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})=\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
.

Consider the diagram (
4.10
). Since its pull-back by
e_{1}^{\ast}
is isomorphic to the diagram for
X^{\prime}/Y/S
, therefore
e_{1}^{\ast}\widetilde{C}^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})=\widetilde{C}^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
.

Now assume the condition (C2) in
4.1
holds for
Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}/Y/S
.
Since
\mathcal{K}_{Z^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}}=\mathcal{K}_{Z^{\prime}/Y}
, the condition (C2)
also holds for
Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S
and then
C^{Z^{\prime}}_{X^{\prime}/Y^{\prime}/S}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
is well-defined.
Now (
4.15.8
) follows from
(
4.15.7
).
∎

4.16.

At last, we show that the non-acyclicity class is compatible with the specialization map.
Consider the commutative diagram (
4.1
).
Assume that
g
is a smooth morphism and that
S
is a henselian trait. Let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
and
s
a geometric point of
S
above its closed point.
We first define a canonical morphism

(4.16.1)

\displaystyle\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}/\eta})\to\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}/s}.

Consider the commutative diagram (
4.2
) and similar diagrams over
\eta
and
s
:

We define
j_{Y}:Y\times_{S}Y\setminus\delta(Y)\to Y\times_{S}Y
to be the open immersion and similar for

and
j_{Y_{s}}
.
Note that
\delta^{\ast}\delta_{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda=\delta^{!}\Lambda
and
\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}
are locally constant on
Y
since
g
is smooth.
Applying
c_{f,{\rm id},-,\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}}
to the distinguished triangle
\delta^{\ast}\delta_{\ast}\delta^{!}\Lambda\to\delta^{\ast}\Lambda\to\delta^{\ast}j_{Y\ast}\Lambda\xrightarrow{+1}
, we get
a commutative diagram between distinguished triangles:

which induces an isomorphism

(4.16.12)

\displaystyle f^{\ast}\delta^{\ast}j_{Y\ast}\Lambda\otimes^{L}f^{!}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}\xrightarrow[\simeq]{c_{f,{\rm id},\delta^{\ast}j_{Y\ast}\Lambda,\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}}}f^{!}(\delta^{\ast}j_{Y\ast}\Lambda\otimes^{L}\mathcal{K}_{Y/S}).

We have similar diagrams for
\Psi_{g}
and
c_{f_{t},{\rm id},-,\mathcal{K}_{Y_{t}/t}}
(
t\in\{\eta,s\}
), which induce the following isomorphisms

(4.16.13)

\displaystyle\Psi_{g}(\mathcal{K}_{Y_{\eta}/\eta}\otimes^{L}\delta_{\eta}^{\ast}j_{Y_{\eta}\ast}\Lambda)\simeq\mathcal{K}_{Y_{s}/s}\otimes^{L}\delta_{s}^{\ast}j_{Y_{s}\ast}\Lambda,

(4.16.14)

\displaystyle f_{t}^{\ast}\delta_{t}^{\ast}j_{Y_{t}\ast}\Lambda\otimes^{L}f_{t}^{!}\mathcal{K}_{Y_{t}/t}\xrightarrow[\simeq]{c_{f_{t},{\rm id},\delta_{t}^{\ast}j_{Y_{t}\ast}\Lambda,\mathcal{K}_{Y_{t}/t}}}f_{t}^{!}(\delta_{t}^{\ast}j_{Y_{t}\ast}\Lambda\otimes^{L}\mathcal{K}_{Y_{t}/t}).

By
4.3
and (
4.16.14
), we get isomorphisms

(4.16.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{K}_{X_{t}/Y_{t}/t}\overset{\ref {remark:expFordeltaDelta}}{\simeq}\mathcal{K}_{X_{t}/t}\otimes^{L}\delta_{t0}^{\ast}(f_{t}\times f_{t})^{\ast}j_{Y_{t}\ast}\Lambda&\simeq f_{t}^{!}\mathcal{K}_{Y_{t}/t}\otimes^{L}f_{t}^{\ast}\delta_{t}^{\ast}j_{Y_{t}\ast}\Lambda\\
&\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{eq:PsiKXYS-2-2}}f_{t}^{!}(\mathcal{K}_{Y_{t}/t}\otimes^{L}\delta_{t}^{\ast}j_{Y_{t}\ast}\Lambda).\end{split}

Similar to the definition of (
3.17.5
), we define (
4.16.1
) to be the following composition

(4.16.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}/\eta})&\overset{\eqref{eq:PsiKXYS-5}}{\simeq}\Psi_{h}(f_{\eta}^{!}(\mathcal{K}_{Y_{\eta}/\eta}\otimes^{L}\delta_{\eta}^{\ast}j_{Y_{\eta}\ast}\Lambda))\\
&\xrightarrow{\rm b.c}f_{s}^{!}\Psi_{g}(\mathcal{K}_{Y_{\eta}/\eta}\otimes^{L}\delta_{\eta}^{\ast}j_{Y_{\eta}\ast}\Lambda)\overset{\eqref{eq:PsiKXYS-3}}{\simeq}f_{s}^{!}(\mathcal{K}_{Y_{s}/s}\otimes^{L}\delta_{s}^{\ast}j_{Y_{s}\ast}\Lambda)\overset{\eqref{eq:PsiKXYS-5}}{\simeq}\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}/s}.\end{split}

Applying
\Psi_{h}:D^{+}(X_{\eta},\Lambda)\to D^{+}(X_{s},\Lambda)
to the following distinguished triangle (cf. (
4.2.13
))

\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}}\to\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/\eta}\to\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}/\eta}\xrightarrow{+1},

we obtain a commutative diagram by the constructions

Similar to (

), the morphism
\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{K}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}/\eta})\to\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}/s}
induces the following specialization maps

Proposition 4.17
.

Under the notation and assumptions in
4.16
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X_{\eta},\Lambda)
such that
f_{\eta}

(
resp.
f_{s})
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}

(
resp.
\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{F}))
outside
Z_{\eta}

(
resp.
Z_{s})
. Then we have

(4.17.1)

\displaystyle{\rm sp}(\widetilde{C}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}/\eta}^{Z_{\eta}}(\mathcal{F}))=\widetilde{C}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}/s}^{Z_{s}}(\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{F}))\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}_{Z_{s}}(X_{s},\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}/s}).

If moreover
H^{0}(Z_{t},\mathcal{K}_{Z_{t}/{Y_{t}}})=0~{\rm and}~H^{1}(Z_{t},\mathcal{K}_{Z_{t}/{Y_{t}}})=0
for
t\in\{s,\eta\}
, then we have

(4.17.2)

\displaystyle{\rm sp}(C^{Z_{\eta}}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}/\eta}(\mathcal{F}))={C}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}/s}^{Z_{s}}(\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{F}))\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}_{Z_{s}}(X_{s},\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/s}).

In particular, if
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that
h
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
and that
f_{\eta}

(
resp.
f_{s})
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X_{\eta}}

(
resp.
\mathcal{F}|_{X_{s}})
outside
Z_{\eta}

(
resp.
Z_{s})
, then
\Psi_{h}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{\eta}})\simeq\mathcal{F}|_{X_{s}}
and hence

(4.17.3)

\displaystyle{\rm sp}(\widetilde{C}_{X_{\eta}/Y_{\eta}/\eta}^{Z_{\eta}}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{\eta}}))=\widetilde{C}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}/s}^{Z_{s}}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{s}})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}_{Z_{s}}(X_{s},\mathcal{K}_{X_{s}/Y_{s}/s}).

Proof.

The equality (
4.17.1
)
results from the following commutative diagram

where the commutativity follows from Proposition

, (

∎

5.
Fibration formula

Inspired by the induction formula for the characteristic classes
[
28
, Proposition 5.3.7]
, we prove the following fibration formula.

Theorem 5.1

(Fibration formula)
.

Under the notation and conditions (C1)-(C3) in
4.1
, we have

(5.1.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})=\delta^{!}C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})+C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S}),

where
\delta^{!}:H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y})\xrightarrow{}H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})
is defined in (
3.12.9
).

When
g:Y\to S
is smooth of relative dimension
r
, we have

\delta^{!}=c_{r}(f^{*}\Omega_{Y/S}^{1,\vee})
by (
3.13.2
). Then (
5.1.1
) can be rewritten as

(5.1.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})&=c_{r}(f^{\ast}\Omega^{1,\vee}_{Y/S})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})+C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S}).\\
\end{split}

In the case that
X=Y\to S={\rm Spec}k
is smooth over a field
k
. Since
{\rm id}:X\setminus Z\to X\setminus Z
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
, the cohomology sheaves of
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
are locally constant on
X\setminus Z
. In this case,
the class
C_{X/Y/S}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
is Abbes-Saito’s localized characteristic class
[
1
, Definition 5.2.1]
and
(
5.1.1
) follows from
[
1
, Proposition 5.2.3]
.

5.2.

Theorem
5.1
will follow from Proposition
5.10
later.
Let us explain the main idea of the proof.
Let
U=X\setminus Z
.
Theorem
3.13
implies that
the image of
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})-\delta^{!}C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})

in
H^{0}(U,\mathcal{K}_{U/S})
is zero.
From the exact sequence

(5.2.1)

\displaystyle H^{-1}(U,\mathcal{K}_{U/S})\to H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/S})\xrightarrow{\tau_{\ast}}H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})\to H^{0}(U,\mathcal{K}_{U/S}),

we have

C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})-\delta^{!}C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})\in{\rm Im}(\tau_{\ast})
.
The lifting of
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})-\delta^{!}C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})

to
H^{0}(Z,\mathcal{K}_{Z/S})
may not be unique. In order to overcome this problem, we enhance the constructions of
C_{X/S}
and
C^{Z}_{X/Y/S}
to the
\infty
-categorical level and construct an intermediate map
L^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})
together with a coherent commutative diagram (cf. (

Since

, the diagram (
5.2
)
implies the fibration formula (
5.1.1
).

5.3.

Let us recall a lifting result in
\infty
-category, which will be used in constructing the map
L^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})
and the diagram (
5.2
).
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category.
Let
\mathcal{E}\subseteq{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})
be the full sub-
\infty
-category spanned by cofiber sequences in
\mathcal{C}
. Let
\theta:\mathcal{E}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})
be the functor sending a cofiber sequence
P^{\prime\prime}\to P\to P^{\prime}
to
P\to P^{\prime}
, which is a trivial Kan fibration by
[
20
, Remark 1.1.1.7]
.

Consider a commutative diagram in
\mathcal{C}
between cofiber sequences

5.4.

), we formulate the following fact.

-category.

which depend on (
5.4
) and (
5.4
) respectively.
Indeed, the diagram (
5.4
) gives a homotopy
gf\simeq gh
, thus

P\xrightarrow{f-h}Q\xrightarrow{g}Q^{\prime}
is homotopic to zero.
This determines the left diagram in (
5.4
).
More precisely, the two diagrams can be constructed as follows.
We denote the composition
Q\oplus Q\simeq Q\amalg Q\xrightarrow{\rm can}Q
again by
{\rm can}
.

5.5.

To construct prisms similar to (
5.4
) in our setting (cf.
5.10
),
we first give a coherent commutative cube in Lemma
5.6
below.
Let
{\rm Corr}_{S}^{\otimes}={\rm Corr}^{\rm proper}_{\rm sep;all}(\rm Sch_{S})
be the symmetric monoidal
(\infty,2)
-category of correspondences (cf.
[
10
, Part III]
).
Let
\mathcal{D}:{\rm Corr}^{\otimes}_{S}\to{\rm Cat}_{\infty}
be a 6-functor formalism of étale sheaves of
\Lambda
-modules on schemes over
S
such that
D(-,\Lambda)=h\mathcal{D}(-)
is the homotopy category of
\mathcal{D}(-)
.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{\rm cons}(-)\subseteq\mathcal{D}(-)
be the full sub-
\infty
-category spanned by perfect-constructible complexes.
For any Noetherian scheme
X
over
S
, we have
D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)=h\mathcal{D}_{\rm cons}(X)
(cf.
[
13
, 7.2]
and
[
12
, §2]
).

In the proof, we will use a result of the exponentiation of cocartesian fibrations
[
19
, Proposition 3.1.2.1]
.
Let
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{C}
be a cocartesian fibration of
\infty
-categories and
K
a simplicial set.
Then
{\rm Fun}(K,\mathcal{E})\to{\rm Fun}(K,\mathcal{C})
is a cocartesian fibration. An edge

f\xrightarrow{e}f^{\prime}
in
{\rm Fun}(K,\mathcal{E})
is a cocartesian edge (for the fibration
{\rm Fun}(K,\mathcal{E})\to{\rm Fun}(K,\mathcal{C})
) if and only if for any vertex
v\in K
,
f(v)\xrightarrow{e(v)}f^{\prime}(v)
is a cocartesian edge in
\mathcal{E}
(for the fibration
\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{C}
).

The following proof is due to Jiangnan Xiong. The main idea is to give a universal characterization of
c_{\delta,p_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
by using cocartesian/cartesian edges.

Proof.

We recall some standard constructions.
By unstraightening
\mathcal{D}
, we have a cocartesian fibration
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda}\to{\rm Corr}_{S}^{\otimes}
. In the following, we will denote a correspondence

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.01389pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.93124pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.3948pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-11.38092pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 18.3948pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-7.01389pt\raise-18.71423pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

simply by
Y\leftarrow C\to X
.
Objects in
{\rm Corr}_{S}^{\otimes}
are given by
(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})

with
n\geq 0
and
X_{i}\in{\rm Sch}_{S}
.
Objects in
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda}
are given by
(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n};\mathcal{F}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{F}_{n})

with
\mathcal{F}_{i}\in\mathcal{D}(X_{i})
.
We have an equivalence

(5.6.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}(X_{i}).

Let
\langle n\rangle=\{\ast,1,\cdots,n\}
.
A morphism
(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})\to(Y_{1},\cdots,Y_{m})
in
{\rm Corr}_{S}^{\otimes}
is given by a map
\alpha:\langle n\rangle\to\langle m\rangle
with
\alpha(\ast)=\ast
and correspondences

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.12067pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.12067pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y_{j}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 66.02617pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 93.60663pt\raise 6.61111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.6389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f_{j}=(f_{ji})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 141.00772pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.62927pt\raise 5.49306pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.52084pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g_{j}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.12068pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 141.00772pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod\limits_{i\in\alpha^{-1}(j)}X_{i}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

for any
1\leq j\leq m
.
For simplicity, we denote such a morphism by
(\alpha,g_{!}f^{*})=(\alpha,(g_{j!}(f_{ji})^{*}))
or by
g_{!}f^{*}=(g_{j!}(f_{ji})^{*})
.
The functor
\mathcal{D}(g_{!}f^{*}):\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}(X_{i})\to\prod\limits_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{D}(Y_{j})

sends
(\mathcal{F}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{F}_{n})
to
(\mathcal{G}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{G}_{m})
with

(5.6.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{j}=g_{j!}f_{j}^{\ast}(\boxtimes^{L}_{i\in\alpha^{-1}(j)}\mathcal{F}_{i})=g_{j!}(\otimes^{L}_{i\in\alpha^{-1}(j)}f_{ji}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}_{i}).

We have a cocartesian edge (for the fibration
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda}\to{\rm Corr}_{S}^{\otimes}
) above
g_{!}f^{*}

(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n};\mathcal{F}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{F}_{n})\to(Y_{1},\cdots,Y_{m};\mathcal{G}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{G}_{m}),

where
\mathcal{G}_{i}
is defined by (
5.6.16
).
If
m=n
and
\alpha:\langle n\rangle\to\langle m\rangle
is the identity, then
the functor

(5.6.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{D}(g_{!}f^{*}):\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}(X_{i})&\to\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}(Y_{i});\quad(\mathcal{F}_{i})\mapsto(g_{i!}f_{i}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}_{i})\end{split}

admits a right adjoint

(5.6.18)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}(Y_{i})&\to\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{D}(X_{i});\quad(\mathcal{G}_{i})\mapsto(f_{i\ast}g_{i}^{!}\mathcal{G}_{i}).\end{split}

In this case, there is a locally cartesian edge

(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n};f_{1\ast}g_{1}^{!}\mathcal{G}_{1},\cdots,f_{n\ast}g_{n}^{!}\mathcal{G}_{n})\to(Y_{1},\cdots,Y_{n};\mathcal{G}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{G}_{n})

above
g_{!}f^{*}:(X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})\to(Y_{1},\cdots,Y_{n})
(cf. the dual version of
[
19
, Corollary 5.2.2.5]
).

Now we go back to the proof of Lemma
5.6
.
For convenience, we will use the arrow
\hookrightarrow
(resp.
\twoheadrightarrow
) to indicate a locally cartesian edge (resp. cocartesian edge).
We first give a universal characterization of
c_{\delta,p_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
.
Indeed, we have a commutative diagram in
{\rm Corr}_{S}^{\otimes}
(cf. the square
WW_{0}TT_{0}
in (
5.5
)):

where
(T,W_{0})\xrightarrow{(i,p_{1})^{\ast}}(W)
is given by the correspondence

W=W\xrightarrow{(i,p_{1})}T\times_{S}W_{0}
,
(W)\xrightarrow{i_{!}}(T)
is given by the correspondence
T\xleftarrow{i}W=W
,
(T,W_{0})\xrightarrow{({\rm id},\delta_{!})}(T,T_{0})
is given by
(T=T=T,T_{0}\xleftarrow{\delta}W_{0}=W_{0})
and
(T,T_{0})\xrightarrow{({\rm id},p_{0})^{\ast}}(T)

is given by
T=T\xrightarrow{({\rm id},p_{0})}T\times_{S}T_{0}
. The two compositions

(T,W_{0})\to(W)=(W)\to(T)\quad{\rm and}\quad(T,W_{0})\to(T,T_{0})\to(T)

are both equal to
i_{!}(i,p_{1})^{*}
, i.e., the correspondence
T\xleftarrow{i}W\xrightarrow{(i,p_{1})}T\times_{S}W_{0}
.
Then we can characterize
c_{\delta,p_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
by using the following lifting problem over (
5.5
):

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern Indeed, the lifting
c^{\prime}
uniquely exists by the property of cocartesian edges (cf.
[
19
, 2.4.1.1]
) and
c_{\delta,p_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
is the unique morphism (up to a contractible space)
making (
5.5
) commutes by the property of cartesian edges.

We denote the diagram (
5.5
) by
\Gamma_{WW_{0}TT_{0}}
.
We also have three similar diagrams
\Gamma_{UW_{0}VT_{0}}
,
\Gamma_{W^{\prime}W_{0}^{\prime}T^{\prime}T_{0}^{\prime}}

and
\Gamma_{U^{\prime}W_{0}^{\prime}V^{\prime}T_{0}^{\prime}}
.
Now we prove Lemma
5.6
by constructing these four diagrams simultaneously.
Here is a brief summary of the notation:

(1)

The diagram
M:\Delta^{2}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
(cf. (
5.5
)) realizes
the bottom lines of these four diagrams at the same time.

(2)

The left (resp. right) vertical arrows of these four diagrams are realized by the diagram
L:\Delta^{1}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
(resp.
R:\Delta^{1}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
).

(3)

The diagram
K:\Delta^{1}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
realizes the diagonal dashed arrows (cf.
c^{\prime}
in (
5.5
)) of these four diagrams.

(4)

The top arrows (cf.
c_{\delta,p_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
in (
5.5
)) of these four diagrams are realized by the diagram
F:\Delta^{1}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
(cf. (
5.5
)). The diagram
F
determines the required commutative diagram (
5.6
) by pulling back to
U^{\prime}
.

More precisely, let
M_{1}:\Delta^{0}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
be the commutative diagram

Let
N_{01}=N|_{\Delta^{\{0,1\}}}
be the left cubic diagram in (
5.5
) and
N_{12}=N|_{\Delta^{\{1,2\}}}
the right cubic diagram.
We can extend
M_{1}
(cf. (
5.5
)) to a locally cartesian edge

M_{01}:\Delta^{\{0,1\}}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
above
N_{01}
, and
extend
M_{1}
to a cocartesian edge
M_{12}:\Delta^{\{1,2\}}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
above
N_{12}
.
Now
M_{01}
and
M_{12}
give a diagram
\Lambda_{1}^{2}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
, which can be extended to a diagram
M:\Delta^{2}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})

Note that
N_{02}=H_{02}
(cf. (
5.5
)).
In the following, we use the standard notation that
\Delta^{2}\xrightarrow{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}\mathcal{C}
means a functor
f:\Delta^{2}\to\mathcal{C}
such that
f|_{\Delta^{\{1,2\}}}=\alpha,f|_{\Delta^{\{0,2\}}}=\beta
and
f|_{\Delta^{\{0,1\}}}=\gamma
.
Let
L:\Delta^{1}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
be a cocartesian lifting over
H_{01}
with
L_{0}=M_{0}
.
By the property of cocartesian edge,
\Lambda_{0}^{2}\xrightarrow{(\bullet,M_{02},L)}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
can be extended to
\Delta^{2}\xrightarrow{(K,M_{02},L)}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
over
H
.
Let
R:\Delta^{1}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
be a locally cartesian lifting over
H_{12}
with
R_{1}=M_{2}
.
Then
\Lambda_{2}^{2}\xrightarrow{(R,K,\bullet)}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
can be extended to
\Delta^{2}\xrightarrow{(R,K,F)}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
.
By construction, the diagram
F
is of the following form

where
A_{0}=H_{1}
and
A_{1}
is the constant functor taking value
(U^{\prime})
.
Put
{\rm Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}={\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}_{S,\Lambda})
and
{\rm Fun}_{S}={\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},{\rm Corr}_{S}^{\otimes})
.
Consider the covariant transport functor along
A

(5.6.71)

\displaystyle A_{!}:{\rm Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}\times_{{\rm Fun}_{S}}\{A_{0}\}\to{\rm Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}\times_{{\rm Fun}_{S}}\{A_{1}\}={\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{D}(U^{\prime})).

The diagram
F
defines an edge
\Delta^{1}\to{\rm Fun}_{\mathcal{C}}\times_{{\rm Fun}_{S}}\{A_{0}\}
.
Now let
G=A_{!}\circ F:\Delta^{1}\to{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1},\mathcal{D}(U^{\prime}))
, which defines a diagram
\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}\to\mathcal{D}(U^{\prime})
. Unwinding the definitions,
G
is the required commutative diagram (
5.6
).
∎

5.7.

We apply Lemma
5.6
to construct the diagram (
5.7
) below, which is crucial for proving the fibration formula (
5.1.1
).
Consider the diagrams (
4.1
) and (
4.2
).
Let
j:U=X\setminus Z\to X
be the open immersion and
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm cons}(X)
. We put

(5.7.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{S}

\displaystyle=R\mathcal{H}om_{X\times_{S}X}({\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}_{1}^{!}\mathcal{F}),\qquad\qquad\mathcal{T}_{S}=\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}),

(5.7.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{S}

\displaystyle=R\mathcal{H}om_{U\times_{S}U}({\rm pr}_{2}^{\ast}j^{\ast}\mathcal{F},{\rm pr}_{1}^{!}j^{\ast}\mathcal{F}),\qquad\;\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{S}=j^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes^{L}_{S}D_{U/S}(j^{\ast}\mathcal{F}).

5.8.

Using the 6-functor formalism
\mathcal{D}:{\rm Corr}^{\otimes}_{S}\to{\rm Cat}_{\infty}
, we can extend the definitions
of
C_{X/S}
(cf. (
3.7.4
) and (
5.8.1
)) and
C^{Z}_{X/Y/S}
(cf.
4.5
and (
5.8.15
)) to objects in
\mathcal{D}_{\rm cons}(X)
satisfying universal local acyclicity conditions
4
4

4

Let
\mathcal{C}_{S,\Lambda}^{\otimes}\to{\rm Corr}_{S}^{\otimes}
be the
unstraightening of
\mathcal{D}:{\rm Corr}_{S}^{\otimes}\to{\rm Cat}_{\infty}
.
A morphism
X\to S
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
an object
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\rm cons}(X)
if and only if
(X,\mathcal{F})
is
dualisable in the symmetric
monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{S,\Lambda}^{\otimes}
(cf.
[
18
, Theorem 2.16]
and
[
12
, Definition 3.2]
).
.
We can also enhance the functor
\delta^{\Delta}
(cf.
2.11
) and
upgrade (
4.2.13
) to be a cofiber sequence in
\mathcal{D}(X)
.
Some diagrams in Section
2

and Section
3
can be upgraded to coherent commutative diagrams in stable
\infty
-categories,
for example, (
3.12
) and (
4.10
).

In the following, we
assume the conditions (C1) and (C2) in
4.1
hold.
We fix an object
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\rm cons}(X)
such that

X\setminus Z\to Y
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
and that
X\to S
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}
.
By the assumptions on
\mathcal{F}
, we have morphisms
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F}):\Lambda\to\mathcal{K}_{X/S}
and
C_{U/Y}(\mathcal{F}):\Lambda\to\mathcal{K}_{U/Y}
.
More precisely, the morphism
C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})
is defined by
the following composition
5
5

5

In an
\infty
-category, the composition of two maps is well-defined up to a contractible space.

(5.8.1)

\displaystyle\Lambda\xrightarrow{\rm id}R\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{eq:keyisoCshrinkRHOM}}\delta_{0}^{!}\mathcal{H}_{S}\xleftarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{eq:Kunethiso}}\delta_{0}^{!}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{}\delta_{0}^{\ast}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{\rm ev}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}.

Let
C_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})
be the following composition (cf. (
4.10
))

(5.8.2)

\displaystyle\Lambda\to\delta^{!}_{0}\mathcal{H}_{S}\xleftarrow{\simeq}\delta_{0}^{!}\mathcal{T}_{S}\simeq\delta_{1}^{!}i^{!}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{}\delta_{1}^{\ast}i^{!}\mathcal{T}_{S}\to\delta_{1}^{\ast}\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{\rm b.c}\delta^{\Delta}\delta_{0}^{\ast}\mathcal{T}_{S}\xrightarrow{\rm ev}\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}=\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S},

which is equivalent to the composition
\Lambda\xrightarrow{C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})}\mathcal{K}_{X/S}\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:distriK}}\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}
.
Similar for
C_{U/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}):=C_{U/Y/S}(\mathcal{F}|_{U})
. We have a commutative diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 46.06659pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.47221pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern \beta
is the homotopy defined by the right squares in (
5.8
),

\gamma
is the homotopy determined by the isomorphism
j_{\ast}\delta_{1}^{\prime\ast}\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{S}\simeq 0
.

5.9.

In the following, let us construct the required map
L^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})
.
We denote by
{\rm Map}(\Lambda,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})={\rm Map}_{\mathcal{D}(X)}(\Lambda,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})
the mapping space whose
\pi_{0}
is
H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S})
.
Since
\pi_{0}({\rm Map}(\Lambda,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}))\simeq\pi_{0}({\rm Map}(\Lambda,\mathcal{K}_{U/Y}))
,
there is a morphism
C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F}):\Lambda\to\mathcal{K}_{X/Y}
with a commutative diagram

In particular,
the composition
\Lambda\xrightarrow{L^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})}\tau_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{Z/S}\to\tau_{\ast}\tau^{!}\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/S}
is homotopic to
C^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})
, which is independent of the choice of
C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})
.
From (
5.10
), we get the following equality:

(5.10.5)

\displaystyle C_{X/S}(\mathcal{F})=\delta^{!}C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})+C^{Z}_{X/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/S}).

Theorem
5.1
follows directly from Proposition
5.10
by
D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)=h\mathcal{D}_{\rm cons}(X)
and the fact that
h:X\to S
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}_{\rm cons}(X)
if and only if
h:X\to S
is universally locally acyclic relatively to the image of
\mathcal{F}
in
D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
(cf.
[
12
, Proposition 3.3]
and
[
19
, 1.2.4]
).

Proof.

).

where the composition
j_{\ast}\Lambda\to j_{\ast}\delta_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{Y}\to j_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{U/Y}
is equal to
j_{\ast}\Lambda\xrightarrow{C_{U/Y}(\mathcal{F})}j_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{U/Y}
, the composition
j_{\ast}\Lambda\to j_{\ast}\delta_{1}^{\prime\ast}i^{\prime!}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{S}\to j_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{U/S}
is equal to
j_{\ast}\Lambda\xrightarrow{C_{U/S}(\mathcal{F})}j_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{U/S}
and
the right face of (
5.9
) is given by Theorem
3.13
(cf. (
3.12
)).
Note that the composition
j_{\ast}\Lambda\to j_{\ast}\delta_{1}^{\prime\ast}\delta^{\Delta}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{S}\to j_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{U/Y/S}
on the front face of (
5.9
) is the morphism
j_{\ast}\Lambda\xrightarrow{C_{U/Y/S}(\mathcal{F})}j_{\ast}\mathcal{K}_{U/Y/S}
(cf. (
5.8.2
)).

5.9
) and (

179.29443pt\raise-48.84279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{j_{*}\mathcal{K}_{U/Y/S}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

Now we show that the top face of (
5.9
) is equal to (
5.8
).
It is enough to compare their right squares.
Let
E
be the right square on the top face of (
5.9
). Consider the notation
\gamma
and
\beta
in (
5.8.20
). Since the homotopies on the top faces of (
5.9
) and (
5.9
) are given by
\beta
and
\gamma^{-1}
respectively,
the homotopy in
E
is equal to the composition of
\beta
and
\gamma=(\gamma^{-1})^{-1}
. Thus
E
is equal to the right square of (
5.8
) by (
5.8.20
).
We remark that the back face of (
5.9
) is equal to (
5.9
) since
the back face of (
5.9
) (resp. (
5.9
)) is equal to the left (resp. right) diagram in (
5.9
).
Finally, the diagram (
5.9
) gives the required diagram (
5.10
). This finishes the proof.
∎

6.
Applications: Milnor formula, conductor formula and Saito’s conjecture

6.1.

In this section, we assume
k
is a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
.
Let
\Lambda
be a finite local ring whose residue field is of characteristic
\ell\neq p
.
We first apply the pull-back property of the non-acyclicity classes to prove a cohomological Milnor formula.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
Y
be a smooth curve over
k
.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a separated morphism of finite type and
x\in|X|
a closed point. Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that
f|_{X\setminus\{x\}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus\{x\}}
.
Then we have

(6.2.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/Y/k}^{\{x\}}(\mathcal{F})=-{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}}(\mathcal{F},f)\quad{\rm in}\quad\Lambda=H^{0}_{x}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}),

where
R\Phi(\mathcal{F},f)
is the complex of vanishing cycles and
{\rm dimtot}={\rm dim}+{\rm Sw}
is the total dimension.

The above theorem gives a cohomological analogue of the Milnor formula proved by Saito in
[
22
, Theorem 5.9]
. Thanks to the cohomological argument, we don’t need the smoothness assumption on
X
.
Before the proof, let us recall Abe’s reformulation of Laumon’s results on local Fourier transform (cf.
[
2
, 6.3]
).
We choose a non-trivial additive character
\psi:\mathbb{F}_{p}\to\Lambda^{\ast}
, and
let
\mathcal{L}
be the Artin-Schreier sheaf on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
associated with
\psi
.
For any morphism
g:X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
, we denote by
\mathcal{L}(g)

the pullback
g^{\ast}\mathcal{L}
.

Theorem 6.3

(Laumon)
.

Let
T=(\mathbb{A}_{k}^{1})_{(0)}

(
resp.
T^{\prime}=(\mathbb{A}_{k}^{1})_{(0^{\prime})})
be
the henselization with coordinate
t

(
resp.
t^{\prime})
. Let
T\xleftarrow{{\rm pr}_{1}}T\times T^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}_{2}}T^{\prime}
be the projections.
For any
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(T,\Lambda)
,
we have

(6.3.1)

\displaystyle-{\rm dimtot}(R\Phi_{\bar{0}}(\mathcal{F},id))={\rm dim}\Psi_{{\rm pr}_{2}}({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime}))_{(\bar{0},\bar{0}^{\prime})},

where
\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime})
is the zero extension to
T\times T^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{L}(t/t^{\prime})
on
T\times T^{\prime}\setminus T\times\{0^{\prime}\}
.

Proof.

For convenience, we record the proof given in
[
2
, 6.3]
.
By applying the reduction map, we may assume that
\Lambda
is a field. Let
i:\{0\}\hookrightarrow T
be the closed point and
j:\eta\hookrightarrow T
the generic point. By
[
28
, 5.2.6]
, there is a finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
with residue field
\Lambda
and a lift
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\eta}\in D_{c}^{b}(\eta,E)
such that the reduction of

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\eta}
is equal to
\mathcal{F}_{\eta}:=j^{\ast}\mathcal{F}
in the Grothendieck group
K_{0}(\eta,\Lambda)
.

Let
\mathfrak{F}^{(0,\infty^{\prime})}(-)
be the functor of local Fourier transform defined in
[
17
, 2.4.2.3]
.
By
[
17
, Proposition 2.4.2.2 and Théorème 2.4.3]
, we have

(6.3.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm dim}\Psi_{{\rm pr}_{2}}({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime}))_{(\bar{0},\bar{0}^{\prime})}&={\rm dim}\Psi_{{\rm pr}_{2}}({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}j_{!}j^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime}))_{(\bar{0},\bar{0}^{\prime})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\rm dim}\Psi_{{\rm pr}_{2}}({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}i_{\ast}i^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(t/t^{\prime}))_{(\bar{0},\bar{0}^{\prime})}\\
&=-{\rm dim}\mathfrak{F}^{(0,\infty^{\prime})}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\eta})+{\rm dim}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{0}}\\
&=-{\rm dimtot}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\eta})+{\rm dim}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{0}}\\
&=-{\rm dimtot}(R\Phi_{\bar{0}}(\mathcal{F},id)).\end{split}

This finishes the proof.
∎

6.4.
Proof of Theorem
6.2

Let
Z=\{x\}
. Since
C^{Z}_{X/Y/k}(\mathcal{F})
depends only on a neighborhood of
Z
by Proposition
4.15
, we may shrink
X
and
Y
for calculating
C_{X/Y/k}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
. Thus we may assume there is an étale morphism
Y\to\mathbb{A}_{k}^{1}
. Since
C_{X/Y/k}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})=C_{X/\mathbb{A}_{k}^{1}/k}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
by Proposition
4.15
, we may reduce the proof to the case where
Y=\mathbb{A}_{k}^{1}
.
Since
Z=\{x\}
, in order to calculate
C_{X/Y/k}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})
,
we may further assume that
k
is algebraically closed.

Now we use the strategy in
[
3
, Theorem 1.6]
.
Let
ft^{\prime}:X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{f\times{\rm id}}\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\mu}\mathbb{A}^{1}
, where
\mu
is the multiplication morphism.
We choose a non-trivial additive character
\psi:\mathbb{F}_{p}\to\Lambda^{\ast}
, and
let
\mathcal{L}
be the Artin-Schreier sheaf on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
associated with
\psi
.
Let
\mathcal{L}_{!}(\mu)
be the zero extension to
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
of
\mu^{\ast}\mathcal{L}
.
Let
\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})
be the zero extension to
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
of
(ft^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathcal{L}
.

We first show that
{\rm pr}_{2}:(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}})\setminus(Z\times\infty)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
{\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})
.
By
[
17
, Théorème 1.3.1.2]
,
{\rm pr}_{2}:\mathbb{A}_{t}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{L}_{!}(\mu)
.
By
[
23
, Proposition 1.4]
,
{\rm pr}_{2}:(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}})\setminus(Z\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}})\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
{\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})
. Since
\mathcal{L}_{!}(\mu)
is locally constant on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
, the morphism
{\rm pr}_{2}:(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}})\setminus(X\times\infty)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
{\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})
. Thus
{\rm pr}_{2}:(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}})\setminus(Z\times\infty)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
{\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})
.

We claim that there exists a pair (
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
) satisfying the following conditions:
6
6

6

When
\Lambda
is a finite field and
\mathcal{F}
is perverse, the claim also follows from
[
25
, Corollary 1.2.3]
.

•

\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
is a finite surjective morphism with
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
integral.

•

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{\prime},\Lambda)
is an extension of
({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})})|_{X\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}\mathbb{P}^{\prime})}
to
X\times\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
.

•

The projection
X\times\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
.

Indeed, this follows from
[
3
, Lemma 1.5]
. Here we include the proof for convenience.
Let
\xi
be an algebraic geometric generic point of
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
. Let
\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}
be the normalization of
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
in
\xi
.
Let
j:X\times\xi\to X\times\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}
be the canonical morphism.
Since
\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}
is absolutely integrally closed, the morphism
{\rm pr}_{2}:X\times\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}\to\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
Rj_{\ast}(({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime}))|_{X\times\xi})
(cf.
[
12
, Corollary 3.10 and Theorem 4.1]
).
Since
Rj_{\ast}(({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime}))|_{X\times\xi})\in D_{\rm ctf}(X\times\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}},\Lambda)
(cf.
[
12
, Corollary 4.2]
),
there is a finite surjective morphism
T\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm c}(X\times T,\Lambda)
such that
\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
factors over
T\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}

and
\mathcal{G}|_{X\times\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}}\simeq Rj_{\ast}(({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime}))|_{X\times\xi})
.
Since
\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}\to T
is surjective,
\mathcal{G}
is also of finite tor-dimension and thus
\mathcal{G}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X\times T,\Lambda)
.
Since
{\rm pr}_{2}:(X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}})\setminus(Z\times\infty)\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
{\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})
, the canonical morphism

\phi:({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})})|_{X\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\times_{\mathbb{P}_{t^{\prime}}^{1}}\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}})}\to Rj_{\ast}(({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime}))|_{X\times\xi})|_{X\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\times_{\mathbb{P}_{t^{\prime}}^{1}}\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}})}\simeq\mathcal{G}|_{X\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\times_{\mathbb{P}_{t^{\prime}}^{1}}\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}})}

is an isomorphism.
Then there is a finite surjective morphism
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\to T
such that
\phi
descends to an isomorphism
{{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})}|_{X\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\times_{\mathbb{P}_{t^{\prime}}^{1}}\mathbb{P}^{\prime})}\to\mathcal{G}|_{X\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\times_{\mathbb{P}_{t^{\prime}}^{1}}\mathbb{P}^{\prime})}
. Put
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}:=\mathcal{G}|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}
.
Since the morphism
{\rm pr}_{2}:X\times\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}\to\overline{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
Rj_{\ast}(({\rm pr}_{1}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime}))|_{X\times\xi})
, the projection
X\times\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
by
[
12
, Theorem 3.7]
.
Thus
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
satisfy the required conditions.

We fix geometric points
0^{\prime}
and
\infty^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
over
0
and
\infty
of
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}
respectively.
Let
\eta^{\prime}
be a geometric generic point of
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
. Let
\Psi_{{\rm pr}^{\prime}_{2}}:=\Psi_{{\rm pr}^{\prime}_{2},\infty^{\prime}\leftarrow\eta^{\prime}}
(cf. (

)).
We have
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}|_{X\times\infty^{\prime}}\simeq\Psi_{\rm pr^{\prime}_{2}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})\simeq\Psi_{\rm pr^{\prime}_{2}}({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})})
, which is supported on
Z
.
We also have
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}|_{X\times 0^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}
. Now we apply Proposition
4.11
and Proposition

to the following diagram

Let
V=\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}}(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}}\setminus\{\infty\})
.
Note that
(X\times V)\setminus(Z\times V)\to Y\times V
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}|_{X\times V}
and
(X\times\infty^{\prime})\setminus(Z\times\infty^{\prime})\to Y\times\infty^{\prime}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\Psi_{\rm pr^{\prime}_{2}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}})
.
Consider the following pull-back morphism and specialization morphism

(6.4.5)

\displaystyle H^{0}(Z\times 0^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{Z\times 0^{\prime}/0^{\prime}})\xleftarrow[\simeq]{{\rm res}_{0}}H^{0}(Z\times V,\mathcal{K}_{Z\times V/V})\xrightarrow[\simeq]{{\rm sp}_{\infty}}H^{0}(Z\times\infty^{\prime},\mathcal{K}_{Z\times\infty^{\prime}/\infty^{\prime}}).

These groups are canonically identified with
\Lambda
.
We have
{\rm res}_{0}(C^{Z\times V}_{X\times V/Y\times V/V}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}))={\rm sp}_{\infty}(C^{Z\times V}_{X\times V/Y\times V/V}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}))
.
By Proposition
4.11
and Proposition
4.17
, we have

(6.4.6)

\displaystyle{\rm res}_{0}(C^{Z\times V}_{X\times V/Y\times V/V}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}))

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:prop:LocBC1}}{=}C^{Z\times{0^{\prime}}}_{X\times 0^{\prime}/Y\times 0^{\prime}/k}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}|_{X\times 0^{\prime}})=C^{Z}_{X/Y/k}(\mathcal{F}),

(6.4.7)

\displaystyle{\rm sp}_{\infty}(C^{Z\times V}_{X\times V/Y\times V/V}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}))

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{spOfNAinpropCor}}{=}C^{Z\times{\infty^{\prime}}}_{X\times\infty^{\prime}/Y\times\infty^{\prime}/k}(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}|_{X\times\infty^{\prime}})=C^{Z}_{X/Y/k}(\Psi_{\rm pr^{\prime}_{2}}({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})})).

Since
\Psi_{\rm pr^{\prime}_{2}}({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})})
is supported on
Z=\{x\}
, we have

(6.4.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}C^{Z}_{X/Y/k}(\Psi_{\rm pr^{\prime}_{2}}({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})}))=\dim\Psi_{\rm pr^{\prime}_{2}}({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})})_{\bar{x}}=-{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}}(\mathcal{F},f).\end{split}

The last equality in (
6.4.8
) follows from
[
2
, Proposition 6.5.2]
. For convenience, we include the proof here.
Let
T=(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}})_{(\bar{\infty})}
be the strict henselization. We consider the following commutative diagram

(6.4.9)

Let
\eta
be the generic point of
T
.
Then we have

(6.4.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Psi_{\rm pr^{\prime}_{2}}({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})})_{\bar{x}}&\overset{(a)}{=}R\Gamma((X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t^{\prime}})_{(\bar{x},\bar{\infty})}\times_{T}\bar{\eta},{{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})})\\
&\overset{(b)}{=}(R\pi_{\eta\ast}Rf^{\prime}_{\eta\ast}({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})}))_{\bar{\eta}}\\
&=(R\pi_{\eta\ast}Rf^{\prime}_{\eta\ast}({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}f^{\prime\ast}_{\eta}\mathcal{L}_{!}(tt^{\prime})}))_{\bar{\eta}}\\
&\overset{(c)}{=}(R\pi_{\eta\ast}(Rf^{\prime}_{\eta\ast}({\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F})\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(tt^{\prime})))_{\bar{\eta}}\\
&\overset{(d)}{=}(R\pi_{\eta\ast}(q^{\ast}Rf_{(\bar{x})\ast}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{!}(tt^{\prime})))_{\bar{\eta}}\\
&\overset{(e)}{=}\Psi_{\pi,\bar{\infty}\leftarrow\bar{\eta}}(q^{\ast}Rf_{(\bar{x})\ast}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{!}(tt^{\prime})),\end{split}

where both
(a)
and (e) follow from the definition of nearby cycles functor (
3.16.5
), (b) follows from the smooth base change, (c) follows from the fact that
f^{\prime\ast}_{\eta}\mathcal{L}_{!}(tt^{\prime})
is a locally constant constructible flat
\Lambda
-modules, (d) follows from the
\Psi
-goodness (cf.
[
15
, Appendix, A.2(1)]
).
Finally, we have

(6.4.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dim\Psi_{\rm pr^{\prime}_{2}}({{\rm pr}_{1}^{\prime\ast}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{L}_{!}(ft^{\prime})})_{\bar{x}}&\overset{\eqref{eq:CalPSIT}}{=}\dim\Psi_{\pi,\bar{\infty}\leftarrow\bar{\eta}}(q^{\ast}Rf_{(\bar{x})\ast}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{!}(tt^{\prime}))\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq:thm:Lau}}{=}-{\rm dimtot}R\Phi(Rf_{(\bar{x})\ast}(\mathcal{F}),id)=-{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}}(\mathcal{F},f).\end{split}

This finishes the proof of Theorem
6.2
.

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
Y
be a smooth connected curve over
k
.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a separated morphism of finite type and
Z\subseteq|X|
be a finite set of closed points.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
such that
f|_{X\setminus Z}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
.
Then we have

(6.5.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})=c_{1}(f^{\ast}\Omega_{Y/k}^{1,\vee})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})-\sum_{x\in Z}{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}}(\mathcal{F},f)\cdot[x]\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}).

Proof.

By (
5.1.2
), we have

(6.5.2)

\displaystyle C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})=c_{1}(f^{\ast}\Omega^{1,\vee}_{Y/k})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})+C_{X/Y/k}^{Z}(\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}).

Now the equality (
6.5.1
) follows from (
6.2.1
).
∎

The following corollary gives a cohomological version of the Grothendieck-Ogg-Shafarevich formula:

Corollary 6.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth connected curve over
k
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
and
Z\subseteq X
be a finite set of closed points such that the cohomology sheaves of
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
are locally constant. Then we have

C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})={\rm rank}\mathcal{F}\cdot c_{1}(\Omega_{X/k}^{1,\vee})-\sum_{x\in Z}{a}_{x}(\mathcal{F})\cdot[x]\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}),

where
a_{x}(\mathcal{F})={\rm rank}\mathcal{F}|_{\bar{\eta}}-{\rm rank}\mathcal{F}_{\bar{x}}+{\rm Sw}_{x}\mathcal{F}
is the Artin conductor and
\eta
is the generic point of
X
.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
6.5
together with the fact that

{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}}(\mathcal{F},{\rm id})=a_{x}(\mathcal{F})
.
∎

Now we apply the proper push-forward property of the non-acyclicity classes to prove a cohomological version of the conductor formula
[
25
, Theorem 2.2.3]
(also of Bloch’s conjecture on the conductor formula for constant sheaves
[
5
]
).

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
Y
be a smooth connected curve over
k
.
Let
f:X\to Y
be a proper morphism over
k
and
y\in|Y|
a closed point. Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
. Assume that
f|_{X\setminus f^{-1}(y)}
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus f^{-1}(y)}
.
Then we have

(6.7.1)

\displaystyle f_{\ast}\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/k}^{f^{-1}(y)}(\mathcal{F})=-{a}_{y}(Rf_{\ast}\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad\Lambda=H^{0}_{y}(Y,\mathcal{K}_{Y/k}).

Proof.

We apply
Proposition
4.14
to the following diagram (take
s=f
and
f^{\prime}={\rm id}
)

\displaystyle\footnotesize\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.12503pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.12503pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 98.18333pt\raise-49.089pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.66112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.12503pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 83.18317pt\raise-62.76506pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.12503pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Spec}k.}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 144.42775pt\raise-62.76506pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.12503pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

Then we get

(6.7.10)

\displaystyle f_{\ast}\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/k}^{f^{-1}(y)}(\mathcal{F})=C^{\{y\}}_{Y/Y/k}(Rf_{\ast}\mathcal{F})\quad{\rm in}\quad\Lambda=H^{0}_{y}(Y,\mathcal{K}_{Y/k}).

Since
f|_{X\setminus f^{-1}(y)}
is proper and universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus f^{-1}(y)}
, the cohomology sheaves of
Rf_{\ast}\mathcal{F}
are locally constant on
Y\setminus\{y\}
.
By the curve case of Theorem
6.2
, we have
C^{\{y\}}_{Y/Y/k}(Rf_{\ast}\mathcal{F})=-{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{y}(Rf_{\ast}\mathcal{F},{\rm id})=-a_{y}(Rf_{\ast}\mathcal{F})
. This proves (
6.7.1
).
∎

Remark 6.8
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a proper flat morphism of smooth schemes over
k
. Let
y\in|Y|
. Assume that
{\rm dim}X=n
,
{\rm dim}Y=1
and that
X\setminus f^{-1}(y)\to Y\setminus\{y\}
is smooth. We expect that

(6.8.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/k}^{f^{-1}(y)}(\Lambda)=(-1)^{n}c_{n,X_{y}}^{X}(\Omega^{1}_{X/Y})\cap[X]\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(f^{-1}(y),\mathcal{K}_{f^{-1}(y)/k}),

where
c_{n,X_{y}}^{X}(\Omega^{1}_{X/Y})
is the localized Chern class defined in
[
5
, Section 1]
. We have the following weaker equality:

(6.8.2)

\displaystyle f_{\ast}(\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/k}^{f^{-1}(y)}(\Lambda))=f_{\ast}((-1)^{n}c_{n,X_{y}}^{X}(\Omega^{1}_{X/Y})\cap[X])\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{K}_{Y/k}).

Indeed, we have

(6.8.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}f_{\ast}(\widetilde{C}_{X/Y/k}^{f^{-1}(y)}(\Lambda))&\overset{\eqref{eq:pushtocurveHelp}}{=}C^{\{y\}}_{Y/Y/k}(f_{\ast}\Lambda)\overset{\eqref{eq:milclass1-1}}{=}C_{Y/k}(f_{\ast}\Lambda)-c_{1}(\Omega_{Y/k}^{1,\vee})\cap C_{Y/Y}(f_{\ast}\Lambda)\\
&=f_{\ast}C_{X/k}(\Lambda)-{\rm rank}(f_{\ast}\Lambda|_{Y\setminus\{y\}})\cdot c_{1}(\Omega_{Y/k}^{1,\vee})\cap C_{Y/Y}(\Lambda)\\
&=f_{\ast}c_{n}(\Omega_{X/k}^{1,\vee})-{\rm rank}(f_{\ast}\Lambda|_{Y\setminus\{y\}})\cdot c_{1}(\Omega_{Y/k}^{1,\vee}).\end{split}

Then (
6.8.2
) follows from the above formula together with
[
25
, Lemma 2.1.3.(2) and Lemma 2.1.4]
.

6.9.

We expect that the non-acyclicity class on a smooth variety can be calculated in
terms of the characteristic cycle. Let
X
be a smooth scheme over
k
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
and let
SS(\mathcal{F})
be the singular support of
\mathcal{F}
. Let
Z\subseteq X
be a closed subscheme and
f:X\to Y={\rm Spec}k[t]
a morphism such that
f
is
SS(\mathcal{F})
-transversal outside
Z
.
By assumption,
df^{-1}(SS(\mathcal{F}))
is supported on
(T^{\ast}Y\times_{Y}Z)\bigcup(T^{\ast}_{Y}Y\times_{Y}X)
,
where
df:T^{\ast}Y\times_{Y}X\to T^{\ast}X
is the canonical morphism induced by
f
.
Consider the section
f^{\ast}dt:X\xrightarrow{dt}T^{\ast}Y\times_{Y}X\xrightarrow{df}T^{\ast}X
.
Then the refined Gysin pull-back of the characteristic cycle
CC(\mathcal{F})
by
f^{\ast}dt

(6.9.1)

\displaystyle cc^{Z}_{X/Y/k}(\mathcal{F}):=(f^{\ast}dt)^{!}(CC(\mathcal{F}))

defines a zero cycle class on
Z
. We expect the following Milnor type formula for non-isolated singular/characteristic points holds.

Conjecture 6.10
.

We have an equality

(6.10.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}^{Z}_{X/Y/k}(\mathcal{F})=\widetilde{{\rm cl}}(cc^{Z}_{X/Y/k}(\mathcal{F}))\qquad{\rm in}\qquad H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/k}),

where
\widetilde{{\rm cl}}
is the composition

CH_{0}(Z)\xrightarrow{\rm cl}H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k})\xrightarrow{\eqref{eq:distriK}}H^{0}_{Z}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y/k})
.

When
Z
is a finite set of closed points, then (
6.10.1
) follows from Theorem
6.2
and
[
22
, Theorem 5.9]
.

6.11.

In the rest of this section, we prove quasi-projective case of Saito’s conjecture
[
22
, Conjecture 6.8.1]
. For the definitions of singular supports and characteristic cycles, we refer to
[
4
]
and
[
22
]
.
Let
X
be a smooth scheme purely of dimension
d
over
k

and
\mathcal{F}\in D_{\rm ctf}(X,\Lambda)
.
Let
Y
be a smooth connected and projective curve over
k
.
Assume that
f:X\to Y
is a good fibration with respect to the singular support

SS(\mathcal{F})
of
\mathcal{F}
, and
Z\coloneq\{x_{v}\}_{v\in\Sigma}
is the set of isolated characteristic points of
f
and
\Sigma\subseteq Y
, i.e., the following conditions hold (cf.
[
28
, Definition 3.4.2]
)

(1)

There exist finitely many closed points
x_{v}(v\in\Sigma)
of
X
such that
f
is

SS(\mathcal{F})
-transversal on
X\setminus Z
(thus
X\setminus Z\to Y
is universally locally acyclic relatively to
\mathcal{F}|_{X\setminus Z}
).

(2)

For each
v\in\Sigma
,
f(x_{v})=v
. If
x_{v}\neq x_{u}
, then
v\neq u
(each fiber
X_{v}
contains at most one isolated characteristic point).

Since
\dim Z=0
, the relative cohomological characteristic class
C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})
is
well-defined (cf. Definition
3.5
).
By Theorem
6.5
,
we have

(6.11.1)

\displaystyle C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})=c_{1}(f^{\ast}\Omega^{1,\vee}_{Y/k})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})-\sum_{v\in\Sigma}{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}_{v}}(\mathcal{F},f)\cdot[x_{v}]\quad{\rm in}\quad H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k}).

Let
\omega
be a non-zero rational 1-form on the curve
Y
such that
\omega
has
neither zeros nor poles on
\Sigma
.
Let
U=X\setminus Z
. Then
the image of
C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})
by the map
H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/Y})\xrightarrow{\simeq}H^{0}(U,\mathcal{K}_{U/Y})
equals

C_{U/Y}(\mathcal{F})
.
We have an equality in
H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{K}_{Y/k})

(6.11.2)

\displaystyle c_{1}(\Omega^{1,\vee}_{Y/k})=-\sum_{v\in|Y\setminus\Sigma|}{\rm ord}_{v}(\omega)\cdot[v].

By Proposition
3.9
, we have

(6.11.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{1}(f^{\ast}\Omega^{1,\vee}_{Y/k})\cap C_{X/Y}(\mathcal{F})=c_{1}(f^{\ast}\Omega^{1,\vee}_{Y/k})\cap C_{U/Y}(\mathcal{F}|_{U})=-\sum_{v\in|Y\setminus\Sigma|}{\rm ord}_{v}(\omega)\cdot C_{X_{v}/v}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{v}}).\end{split}

By (
6.11.1
) and (
6.11.3
), we get the following induction formula for the cohomological characteristic classes:

(6.11.4)

\displaystyle C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{v\in|Y\setminus\Sigma|}{\rm ord}_{v}(\omega)\cdot C_{X_{v}/v}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{v}})-\sum_{v\in\Sigma}{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}_{v}}(\mathcal{F},f)\cdot[x_{v}].

The formula (
6.11.4
) implies that the characteristic class
C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})
can be built from characteristic classes
C_{X_{v}/v}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{v}})
on schemes
X_{v}
of dimension smaller than
X
. Based on this idea, let us explain that Theorem
6.5
implies the quasi-projective case of Saito’s conjecture
[
22
, Conjecture 6.8.1]
7
7

7

We refer to
[
28
]
for a weak version of Saito’s another conjecture
[
22
, Conjecture 6.8.2]
.
.

Conjecture 6.12

(Saito,
[
22
, Conjecture 6.8.1]
)
.

Let
X
be a closed sub-scheme of a smooth scheme over
k
. Let
cc_{X}:=cc_{X,0}:K_{0}(X,\Lambda)\to{\rm CH}_{0}(X)
be the morphism defined in
[
22
, Definition 6.7.2]
.
Then we have a commutative diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 23.68748pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.68748pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 31.52551pt\raise 5.00972pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{cc_{X}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 58.95903pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise where
{\rm cl}:{\rm CH}_{0}(X)\to H^{0}(X,\mathcal{K}_{X/k})
is the cycle class map.

Theorem 6.13
.

Conjecture
6.12
holds for any smooth and quasi-projective scheme
X
over
k
.

Proof.

We prove the theorem by induction on the dimension
d={\rm dim}(X)
.
If
d=0
, then both
C_{X/k}
and
cc_{X}
equal the rank function.
If
d=1
, the result follows from Corollary
6.6
and the Grothendieck-Ogg-Shafarevich formula (cf.
[
22
, Lemma 5.11.3]
). Suppose that
d\geq 1
and that
X
has a good fibration
f:X\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
with respect to
SS(\mathcal{F})
. We use the notation in
6.11
. By
[
28
, Proposition 5.3.7]
, the characteristic class
cc_{X}(\mathcal{F})
satisfies the following fibration formula

(6.13.1)

\displaystyle cc_{X}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{v\in|Y\setminus\Sigma|}{\rm ord}_{v}(\omega)\cdot cc_{X_{v}}(\mathcal{F}|_{X_{v}})-\sum_{v\in\Sigma}{\rm dimtot}R\Phi_{\bar{x}_{v}}(\mathcal{F},f)\cdot[x_{v}]\quad{\rm in}\quad{\rm CH}_{0}(X).

By the induction hypothesis and (
6.11.4
), we get
{\rm cl}(cc_{X}(\mathcal{F}))=C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F})
. Thus the result holds when
X
has a good fibration.

In general, for a finite Galois extension
k^{\prime}/k
of degree invertible in
\Lambda
, assume the result holds for
(X_{k^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}|_{X_{k^{\prime}}})
. We show the result also holds for
(X,\mathcal{F})
.
Indeed, let
\sigma:X_{k^{\prime}}\to X
be the projection. Since
\sigma_{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}^{\oplus{{\rm deg}(k^{\prime}/k)}}
, we get

(6.13.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm deg}(k^{\prime}/k)\cdot{\rm cl}(cc_{X}(\mathcal{F}))&={\rm cl}(cc_{X}(\sigma_{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{F}))\\
&\overset{(a)}{=}\sigma_{\ast}({\rm cl}(cc_{X_{k^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{F})))\\
&=\sigma_{\ast}(C_{X_{k^{\prime}}/k}(\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{F}))\overset{\ref {prop:pushccc}}{=}C_{X/k}(\sigma_{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}\mathcal{F})={\rm deg}(k^{\prime}/k)\cdot C_{X/k}(\mathcal{F}),\end{split}

where
(a)
follows from
[
24
, Lemma 2]
.
Since
{\rm deg}(k^{\prime}/k)
is invertible in
\Lambda
, hence
{\rm cl}(cc_{X}(\mathcal{F}))=C_{X}(\mathcal{F})
.

By
[
28
, Lemma 4.2.7]
, after taking a finite extension of
k
if necessary, we may assume
there exist a blow-up
\pi:\widetilde{X}\to X
of
X
along a closed subscheme of codimension 2 and a good fibration
f:\widetilde{X}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Since
\widetilde{X}
has a good fibration, the result holds for
(\widetilde{X},\pi^{\ast}\mathcal{F})
. By Lemma
3.15
, the result also holds for
(X,\mathcal{F})
. This finishes the proof.
∎
